\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle d\cdot D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{0}(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})

for all
\bm{y}\in R_{d}\oplus\rho
and
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
.

Given a matrix-valued function
B:\mathbb{R}^{D}\to\mathbb{R}^{m\times n}
, its gradient
\nabla B(\bm{x})
will be an
m\times n\times D
array defined as
\left[B(\bm{x})\right]_{ijk}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{k}}B(\bm{x})_{ij}
.
The derivative of
B
in the directional of a vector
\bm{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
is defined as:

B^{\prime}(\bm{x};\bm{z})\equiv\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{B(\bm{x}+\epsilon\bm{z})-B(\bm{x})}{\epsilon}=\nabla B(\bm{x})\bm{z}.

When the matrix
A(\bm{x})=\nabla\nabla f(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we will use the notation
\nabla\nabla f^{\prime}(\bm{x};\bm{z})=\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\bm{z}\equiv\bm{z}^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})
interchangeably to denote its directional derivative along
\bm{z}
.

Recall that an order-
d
ridge of the density
p
in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
is the collection of points defined as:

R_{d}=\left\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}:G_{d}(\bm{x})=\bm{0},\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x})<0\right\}=\left\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}:V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})=\bm{0},\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x})<0\right\}.

Lemma
C.1
below shows that under conditions (A1-3), the Jacobian matrix
\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]
has rank
D-d
at every point of
R_{d}
, and
R_{d}
is a
d
-dimensional manifold by the implicit function theorem
(
Rudin 1976
)
. Consequently, the row space of
\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{(D-d)\times D}
spans the normal space to
R_{d}
.

If we define
M(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]^{T}=\left[\bm{m}_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\bm{m}_{D}(\bm{x})\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times(D-d)}
, the derivation in pages 60-63 of
Eberly 1996
shows that

\bm{m}_{k}(\bm{x})=\left[\lambda_{k}(\bm{x})\bm{I}_{D}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})}{\lambda_{k}(\bm{x})-\lambda_{i}(\bm{x})}\cdot\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})\right]\bm{v}_{k}(\bm{x})

(50)

for
k=d+1,...,D
, and the column space of
M(\bm{x})
spans the normal space to
R_{d}
. Let

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\lambda_{D}(\bm{x})\right],

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x})-\lambda_{i}(\bm{x})},...,\frac{1}{\lambda_{D}(\bm{x})-\lambda_{i}(\bm{x})}\right],

\displaystyle T_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\left[\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})

for
i=1,...,d
. Then,

M(\bm{x})=V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x}).

(51)

However, the columns of
M(\bm{x})
are not orthonormal. Thus, we leverage the orthonormalization in
Chen et al. 2015
to construct
N(\bm{x})
whose columns are orthonormal and span the same column space as
M(\bm{x})
in the following steps. Under the condition that
M(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
has full rank
D-d
at every point
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
(see Lemma
C.1
),
M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})
is positive definite, and we perform the Cholesky decomposition on it, that is,

M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})=J(\bm{x})J(\bm{x})^{T},

(52)

where
J(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{(D-d)\times(D-d)}
is a lower triangular matrix whose diagonal elements are positive. We then define

N(\bm{x})=M(\bm{x})\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}.

(53)

Notice that
M(\bm{x}),N(\bm{x}),J(\bm{x})
intrinsically depend on the dimension
d
of the ridge
R_{d}
, but we do not explicate these dependencies in their notations. As discussed in
Chen et al. 2015
,
M(\bm{x})
might not be unique because the eigenvalues of
\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})
can have their multiplicities greater than 1. Any collection of linearly independent unit eigenvectors of
\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})
fits into the above construction for
M(\bm{x})
. However, as will be shown later, this volatility of
M(\bm{x})
will not affect our results, as we only require the smoothness of
M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})
to develop a lower bound of
\mathtt{reach}(R_{d})
.

Lemma C.1
.

Assume conditions (A1-3). Given that
M(\bm{x})
and
N(\bm{x})
are defined in (
51
) and (
53
), we have the following properties:

(a)

N(\bm{x})
and
M(\bm{x})
have the same column space. In addition,

N(\bm{x})N(\bm{x})^{T}=M(\bm{x})\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}.

That is,
N(\bm{x})N(\bm{x})^{T}
is the projection matrix onto the columns of
M(\bm{x})
.

(b)

The columns of
N(\bm{x})
are orthonormal to each other.

(c)

For
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
, the column space of
N(\bm{x})
is normal to the (tangent) direction of
R_{d}
at
\bm{x}
.

(d)

For all
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
,
\mathtt{rank}(N(\bm{x}))=\mathtt{rank}(M(\bm{x}))=D-d
. Moreover,
R_{d}
is a
d
-dimensional manifold that contains neither intersections and nor endpoints. Namely,
R_{d}
is a finite union of connected and compact manifolds.

(e)

For
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
, all the
(D-d)
nonzero singular values of
M(\bm{x})
are greater than
\beta_{1}>0
and therefore,

\left|\left|\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{1}^{2}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left|\left|\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{1}}.

(f)

When
\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}
is sufficiently small and
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in R_{d}\oplus\rho
,

\left|\left|N(\bm{x})N(\bm{x})^{T}-N(\bm{y})N(\bm{y})^{T}\right|\right|_{\max}\leq A_{0}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}

for some constant
A_{0}>0
.

(g)

Assume that another density function
q
also satisfies conditions (A1-3) and
\left|\left|p-q\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}
is sufficiently small. Then

\left|\left|N_{p}(\bm{x})N_{p}(\bm{x})^{T}-N_{q}(\bm{x})N_{q}(\bm{x})^{T}\right|\right|_{\max}\leq A_{1}\cdot\left|\left|p-q\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}

for some constant
A_{1}>0
and any
\bm{x}\in R_{d}
, where
N_{p}(\bm{x})
is the matrix defined in (
53
) with the underlying density
p
.

(h)

The reach of
R_{d}
satisfies

\mathtt{reach}(R_{d})\geq\min\left\{\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{A_{2}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)}\right\}

for some constant
A_{2}>0
.

Lemma
C.1
is extended from Lemma 2 in
Chen et al. 2015
to handle the density ridge
R_{d}
with
1\leq d<D
. As our conditions (A1-3) imply the imposed conditions of Lemma 2 in
Chen et al. 2015
, our proof of Lemma
C.1
essentially follows from their arguments with some minor modifications.

We adopt and generalize parts of the proof of Lemma 2 in
Chen et al. 2015
.

(a) This property is a natural corollary of the Cholesky decomposition as:

N(\bm{x})N(\bm{x})^{T}=M(\bm{x})\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}J(\bm{x})^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}=M(\bm{x})\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}.

(b) Some direct calculations show that

\displaystyle N(\bm{x})^{T}N(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=J(\bm{x})^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=J(\bm{x})^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}J(\bm{x})^{-1}J(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=J(\bm{x})^{-1}M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}J(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\bm{I}_{D-d}.

(c) It can be proved by the argument of Lemma 1 in
Chen et al. 2015
. Or, we define an arbitrary parametrized curve
\gamma:(-\epsilon,\epsilon)\to\mathbb{R}^{D}
lying within
R_{d}
for some
\epsilon>0
. Then
\gamma^{\prime}(t)
aligns with the tangent direction at
\gamma(t)\in R_{d}
. Since
V_{d}(\gamma(t))^{T}\nabla p(\gamma(t))=\bm{0}
, taking the derivative with respect to
t
gives us that

0=\frac{d}{dt}\left[V_{d}(\gamma(t))^{T}\nabla p(\gamma(t))\right]=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\gamma^{\prime}(t)

with
\bm{x}=\gamma(t)
. Hence, by the arbitrariness of
\gamma(t)
, the column of
M(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
is normal to the tangent direction of
R_{d}
at
\bm{x}
.

(d) We prove that the
(D-d)
nonzero singular values of
M(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times(D-d)}
are bounded away from 0. Recall that

M(\bm{x})=V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})

with

\displaystyle\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\lambda_{D}(\bm{x})\right],

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\frac{1}{\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x})-\lambda_{i}(\bm{x})},...,\frac{1}{\lambda_{D}(\bm{x})-\lambda_{i}(\bm{x})}\right],

\displaystyle T_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\left[\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})

for
i=1,...,d
. Under conditions (A2-3),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|\left|T_{i}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}\quad\text{ by condition (A2)}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|\left|\left[\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\bm{v}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}\quad\text{ since }\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}=1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{0}-\beta_{1}\quad\text{ by condition (A3)}.

It shows that all the singular values of
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})
are less than
\beta_{0}-\beta_{1}
. Moreover, under condition (A2) again, all the
(D-d)
nonzero singular values of
V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})
are greater than
\beta_{0}
. By Theorem 3.3.16 in
Horn and Johnson 1991
, we know that all the
(D-d)
nonzero singular values of
M(\bm{x})
are greater than
\beta_{0}-(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})=\beta_{1}>0
. Therefore,
\mathtt{rank}(M(\bm{x}))=\mathtt{rank}(N(\bm{x}))=D-d
. The rest of the proof follows directly from Claim 4 in
Chen et al. 2015
.

(e) By the proof of (d), we already know that all the
(D-d)
nonzero singular values of
M(\bm{x})
are greater than
\beta_{1}>0
. Thus,
\left|\left|\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{1}^{2}}
, and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left[J(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}\left(J(\bm{x})J(\bm{x})^{T}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}\left(M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right)}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\left|\left|\left[M(\bm{x})^{T}M(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\beta_{1}},

where
\sigma_{\min}(A)
is the smallest singular value of matrix
A
.

Finally, the proofs of properties (f), (g), and (h) are essentially the same as the corresponding claims in
Chen et al. 2015
. We thus omitted them.
∎

As we have discussed in Remark
4.1
, property (d) of Lemma
C.1
demonstrates that our imposed assumptions (A1-3) for the ridge
R_{d}
is sufficient to imply the critical full-rank condition of its normal space in
Chen 2020
in order for
R_{d}
to be a well-defined solution manifold.

D
Proofs of Lemma
3.2
, Proposition
3.3
, and Theorem
3.6

Lemma 3.2
.

Assume conditions (A1) and (E1). The convergence rate of
\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})=\frac{2c_{k,D}}{nh^{D+2}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}-k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\frac{\bm{x}-\bm{X}_{i}}{h}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)
is

\displaystyle h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=-2c_{k,D}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)d\bm{u}+O\left(h^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D}}}\right)

\displaystyle=O(1)+O\left(h^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D}}}\right)

for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
as
nh^{D}\to\infty
and
h\to 0
.

Another interpretation of Lemma
3.2
is that
\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})
diverges to infinity at the rate

O\left(h^{-2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D+4}}}\right)=O\left(n^{\frac{2}{D+4}}\right)+O_{P}\left(1\right)

if we select the bandwidth
h_{\text{opt}}\asymp O\left(n^{-\frac{1}{D+4}}\right)
to minimize the asymptotic mean integrated square error
(
Chen 2017
)
, where “
\asymp
” stands for the asymptotic equivalence.

Note that

h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})=\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]+h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right].

(54)

Given the differentiability of
p
guaranteed by condition (A1), the expectation of
h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})
is given by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2c_{k,D}}{h^{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}-k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\frac{\bm{x}-\bm{y}}{h}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot p(\bm{y})d\bm{y}

\displaystyle=2c_{k,D}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}-k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)\cdot p(\bm{x}+h\bm{u})d\bm{u}\quad\text{ by }\bm{u}=\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{h}

\displaystyle=2c_{k,D}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}-k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)\left[p(\bm{x})+h\nabla p(\bm{x})^{T}\bm{u}+O(h^{2})\right]d\bm{u}

\displaystyle=2c_{k,D}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}-k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)d\bm{u}+O(h^{2}).

By condition (E1), the dominating constant
-2c_{k,D}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)d\bm{u}
is finite and therefore,

\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]=-2c_{k,D}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)d\bm{u}+O\left(h^{2}\right)=O(1)+O(h^{2}).

(55)

In addition, we calculate the variance of
\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})
as

\displaystyle\text{Var}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{k,D}^{2}}{nh^{2D}}\cdot\text{Var}\left[k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\frac{\bm{x}-\bm{X}_{1}}{h}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{k,D}^{2}}{nh^{2D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\frac{\bm{x}-\bm{y}}{h}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}p(\bm{y})\,d\bm{y}-\frac{1}{n}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{k,D}^{2}}{nh^{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\cdot p(\bm{x}+h\bm{u})d\bm{u}-\frac{1}{n}\left[O(1)+O\left(h^{2}\right)\right]^{2}\quad\text{ by }\bm{u}=\frac{\bm{y}-\bm{x}}{h}

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{k,D}^{2}}{nh^{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}\left[p(\bm{x})+h\nabla p(\bm{x})^{T}\bm{u}+O(h^{2})\right]d\bm{u}+o\left(\frac{1}{nh^{D}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{k,D}^{2}\cdot p(\bm{x})}{nh^{D}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}d\bm{u}+o\left(\frac{1}{nh^{D}}\right),

Again, by condition (E1), the dominating constant
4c_{k,D}^{2}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)^{2}d\bm{u}
is finite. Thus, by the central limit theorem,

\displaystyle\begin{split}h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]&=\sqrt{\text{Var}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]}\cdot\frac{h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})-\mathbb{E}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]}{\sqrt{\text{Var}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]}}\\
&=\sqrt{\text{Var}\left[h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})\right]}\cdot\bm{Z}_{n}(\bm{x})\\
&=O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D}}}\right),\end{split}

(56)

where
\bm{Z}_{n}(\bm{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{\to}}\mathcal{N}_{D}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{D})
. Combining (
54
), (
55
), and (
56
), we conclude that

\displaystyle h^{2}\widehat{g}_{n}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=-2c_{k,D}\cdot p(\bm{x})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{D}}k^{\prime}\left(\left|\left|\bm{u}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)+O\left(h^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D}}}\right)

\displaystyle=O(1)+O\left(h^{2}\right)+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{1}{nh^{D}}}\right)

for any
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
as
nh^{D}\to\infty
and
h\to 0
.
∎

Remark D.1
.

Some previous research papers on the mean shift algorithm
Li et al. 2007
;
Carreira-Perpiñán 2007
;
Aliyari Ghassabeh 2015
;
Arias-Castro et al. 2016
have already justified that the algorithm converges to a local mode of the KDE
\widehat{p}_{n}
when its local modes are isolated and the algorithm starts within some small neighborhoods of these estimated local modes.
Lemma
3.2
here provides a (probabilistic) perspective on the linear convergence of the mean shift algorithm.
It is well-known that the set of the true local modes of
p
can be approximated by the set of estimated modes defined by
\widehat{p}_{n}

(
Chen et al. 2016
)
. Moreover, around the true local modes of the density
p
, one can argue that
\widehat{p}_{n}
is strongly convex and has a Lipschitz gradient with probability tending to 1 by the uniform consistency of
\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}
and
\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}
as
h\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{D+4}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
; see the uniform bounds (
16
). Hence, by some standard results in optimization theory (
e.g.
, Chapter 3 in
Bubeck 2015
), a sample-based gradient ascent algorithm with objective function
\widehat{p}_{n}
converges linearly to (estimated) local modes around their neighborhoods as long as its step size is below some threshold value. Finally, recall that (i) the mean shift algorithm is a special variant of the sample-based gradient ascent method with an adaptive size
\eta_{n,h}^{(t)}
by (
19
) and (ii)
\eta_{n,h}^{(t)}
can be sufficiently small but bounded away from 0 when
nh^{D}
is large and
h
is small by Lemma
3.2
; see also Remark
3.2
. Therefore, the mean shift algorithm will converge linearly with high probability around some small neighborhood of the local modes of
\widehat{p}_{n}
when the sample size
n
is sufficiently large and
h
is chosen to be small.

Proposition 3.3

(Convergence of the SCGA Algorithm)
.

For any SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
defined by (
23
) with
0<\eta<\frac{2}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
, the following properties hold.

(a)

Under condition (A1), the objective function sequence
\big\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is non-decreasing and converges.

(b)

Under condition (A1),
\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}=0
.

(c)

Under conditions (A1-3),
\lim_{t\to\infty}d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})=0
whenever
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{1}
with the convergence radius
r_{1}
satisfying

0<r_{1}<\min\left\{\frac{\rho}{2},\;\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{A_{2}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)},\;\frac{\beta_{1}}{A_{4}(p)}\right\},

where
A_{2}>0
is a constant defined in (h) of Lemma
C.1
while
A_{4}(p)>0
is a quantity depending on both the dimension
D
and functional norm
\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
up to the fourth-order (partial) derivatives of
p
.

(a) We first derive the following fact about the objective function
p
.

\bullet

Fact 1
. Given (A1),
p
is
D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-smooth, that is,
\nabla p
is
D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-Lipschitz.

This fact follows from the differentiability of
p
ensured by condition (A1) and Taylor’s theorem that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{x})-\nabla p(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\nabla\nabla p(\widetilde{\bm{y}})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}\leq D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}

for any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, where
\widetilde{\bm{y}}
is within a
\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}
-neighborhood of
\bm{y}
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|p(\bm{y})-p(\bm{x})-\nabla p(\bm{x})^{T}(\bm{y}-\bm{x})\right|&=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla p(\bm{x}+t(\bm{y}-\bm{x}))^{T}(\bm{y}-\bm{x})dt-\nabla p(\bm{x})^{T}(\bm{y}-\bm{x})\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{x}+t(\bm{y}-\bm{x}))-p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{y}-\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}dt\\
&\leq\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{y}-\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}.\end{split}

(57)

When
0<\eta<\frac{2}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\\
&=p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\eta\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\\
&\geq\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\cdot\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\quad\text{ by \eqref{L_smoothness}}\\
&=\eta\left(1-\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\eta}{2}\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\geq 0,\end{split}

(58)

showing that the objective function
p
is non-decreasing along
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. Given the boundedness of
p
guaranteed by condition (A1), we know that the sequence
\left\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is bounded. Thus,
\left\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
also converges.

(b) From (a), we know that when
0<\eta<\frac{2}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
,

\displaystyle p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\eta\left(1-\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\eta}{2}\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2-D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\eta}{2\eta}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}.

Since the sequence
\left\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
converges as
t\to\infty
, it follows that

\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}=0.

(c) Given condition (A2) and the fact that
r_{1}<\frac{\rho}{2}
, we know that

\bm{x}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{1}\text{ and }\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}=0\quad\text{ if and only if}\quad\bm{x}\in R_{d}.

Let
\bm{z}^{(t)}=\pi_{R_{d}}(\bm{x}^{(t)})
denote the projection of
\bm{x}^{(t)}
in the SCGA sequence onto the ridge
R_{d}
. Since
r_{1}\leq\mathtt{reach}(R_{d})
by (h) of Lemma
C.1
,
\bm{z}^{(t)}
is well-defined when
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{1}
. Given that the definition of
M(\bm{z}^{(t)})=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{z}^{(t)})\right]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times(D-d)}
in (
51
), we know that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\\
&=\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\underbrace{V_{d}(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{z}^{(t)})}_{=0}\right|\right|_{2}\\
&=\left|\left|\int_{0}^{1}M\left(\bm{z}^{(t)}+\epsilon(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\right)^{T}(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\,d\epsilon\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ by Taylor's theorem}\\
&=\left|\left|M(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})+\int_{0}^{1}\left[M\left(\bm{z}^{(t)}+\epsilon(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\right)-M(\bm{z}^{(t)})\right]^{T}(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\,d\epsilon\right|\right|_{2}\\
&\geq\left|\left|M(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\epsilon\in[0,1]}\left|\left|\nabla M\left(\bm{z}^{(t)}+\epsilon(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\right)^{T}\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\geq}}\beta_{1}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}-\frac{A_{4}(p)}{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\\
&=d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})\cdot\left(\beta_{1}-\frac{A_{4}(p)}{2}d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})\right)\\
&\geq\frac{\beta_{1}}{2}\cdot d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})\end{split}

(59)

when
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{1}
, where we leverage (e) of Lemma
C.1
to obtain the inequality (i). More specifically,
M(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}
is a full row rank matrix by (d) of Lemma
C.1
and
\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}
lies within the row space of
M(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}
because
\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}
is normal to
R_{d}
at
\bm{z}^{(t)}
. Since the nonzero singular values of
M(\bm{z}^{(t)})
are lower bounded by
\beta_{1}>0
, it follows that
\left|\left|M(\bm{z}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\geq\beta_{1}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}
. From the above derivation, we also know that
A_{4}(p)>0
is indeed the supremum norm of
\nabla M(\bm{x})^{T}=\nabla\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
over the line segment connecting
\bm{x}^{(t)}
and
\bm{z}^{(t)}
, which depends on the uniform functional norm
\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
of the partial derivatives of
p
up to the fourth order. The result follows from (b).
∎

The following Davis-Kahan theorem
(
Davis and Kahan 1970
)
is one of the most notable theorems in matrix perturbation theory. We present the theorem in a modified version from
von Luxburg 2007
;
Genovese et al. 2014
. Other useful variants of the Davis-Kahan theorem can be found in
Yu et al. 2014
.

Lemma D.1
(Davis-Kahan).

Let
H
and
\widetilde{H}
be two symmetric matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, whose spectra (Definition 1.1.4 in
Horn and Johnson 2012
) are
\sigma(H)
and
\sigma(\widetilde{H})
, and
S_{1}\subset\mathbb{R}
be an interval. Denote by
\sigma_{S_{1}}(H)
the set of eigenvalues of
H
that are contained in
S_{1}
, and by
V_{1}
the matrix whose columns are the corresponding (unit) eigenvectors to
\sigma_{S_{1}}(H)
(more formally,
V_{1}
is the image of the spectral projection induced by
\sigma_{S_{1}}(H)
). Denote by
\sigma_{S_{1}}(\widetilde{H})
and
\widetilde{V}_{1}
the analogous quantities for
\widetilde{H}
. If

\delta:=\inf\left\{|\lambda-\widetilde{\lambda}|:\lambda\in\sigma_{S_{1}}(H),\widetilde{\lambda}\in\sigma(\widetilde{H})\setminus\sigma_{S_{1}}(\widetilde{H})\right\}>0,

then the distance
d(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1}):=\left|\left|\sin\bm{\Theta}(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\right|\right|
between two subspaces is bounded by

d(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\leq\frac{\left|\left|H-\widetilde{H}\right|\right|}{\delta}

for any orthogonally invariant norm
\left|\left|\cdot\right|\right|
, such as the Frobenius norm
\left|\left|\cdot\right|\right|_{F}
and the
L_{2}
-operator norm
\left|\left|\cdot\right|\right|_{2}
, where
\bm{\Theta}(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})
is a diagonal matrix with the ascending principal angles between the column spaces of
V_{1}
and
\widetilde{V}_{1}
on the diagonal.

Note that when we take the Frobenius norm in Lemma
D.1
,
\left|\left|\sin\bm{\Theta}(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\right|\right|_{F}=\frac{\left|\left|V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-\widetilde{V}_{1}\widetilde{V}_{1}^{T}\right|\right|_{F}}{\sqrt{2}}
by some simple algebra. Consequently, we will utilize the following inequality from the Davis-Kahan theorem in our subsequent proofs as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\left|V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-\widetilde{V}_{1}\widetilde{V}_{1}^{T}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left|\left|V_{1}V_{1}^{T}-\widetilde{V}_{1}\widetilde{V}_{1}^{T}\right|\right|_{F}=\sqrt{2}\left|\left|\sin\bm{\Theta}(V_{1},\widetilde{V}_{1})\right|\right|_{F}&\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\left|\left|H-\widetilde{H}\right|\right|_{F}}{\delta}\\
&\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|H-\widetilde{H}\right|\right|_{\max}}{\delta}\\
&\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|H-\widetilde{H}\right|\right|_{2}}{\delta}.\end{split}

(60)

Theorem 3.6

(Linear Convergence of the SCGA Algorithm)
.

Assume conditions (A1-4) throughout the theorem.

(a)

Q-Linear convergence of
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}
: Consider a convergence radius
r_{2}>0
satisfying

0<r_{2}<\min\left\{\frac{\rho}{2},\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}}{A_{2}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)},\frac{\beta_{1}}{A_{4}(p)},\frac{3\beta_{0}}{4D\left(6\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+D^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}\right)}\right\},

where
A_{2}>0
is the constant defined in (h) of Lemma
C.1
and
A_{4}(p)>0
is a quantity defined in (c) of Proposition
3.3
that depends on both the dimension
D
and the functional norm
\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
up to the fourth-order derivative of
p
. Whenever
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and the initial point
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
with
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
, we have that

\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ with }\quad\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}}.

(b)

R-Linear convergence of
d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})
: Under the same radius
r_{2}>0
in (a), we have that whenever
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and the initial point
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
with
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
,

d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ with }\quad\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}}.

We further assume conditions (E1-2) in the rest of statements. If
h\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{D+4}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
,

(c)

Q-Linear convergence of
\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}
: under the same radius
r_{2}>0
and
\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}}
in (a), we have that

\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}}\right)

with probability tending to 1 whenever
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and the initial point
\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
with
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
.

(d)

R-Linear convergence of
d_{E}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)},R_{d})
: under the same radius
r_{2}>0
and
\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}}
in (a), we have that

d_{E}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)},R_{d})\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}}\right)

with probability tending to 1 whenever
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and the initial point
\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
with
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
.

The entire proof is inspired by some standard results in optimization theory. However, the objective function
p
is no longer strongly concave, and we focus on the SCGA iteration instead of the standard gradient ascent method. We first recall the following two facts.

\bullet

Fact 1
. Given condition (A1),
p
is
D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-smooth, that is,
\nabla p
is
D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-Lipschitz.

\bullet

Fact 2
. Given conditions (A1-3), we know that
\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}>0
for any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})\setminus R_{d}
and

p(\bm{x}^{*})-p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)\geq 0

for any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
.

Fact 1
has been proved in Proposition
3.3
, implying that the objective function sequence
\left\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is non-decreasing when
\eta<\frac{2}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
.

Fact 2
is a natural corollary by Proposition
3.3
, because
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
and
p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)
is the objective function value after one-step SCGA iteration with step size
\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
. The iteration will move
\bm{x}^{(t)}
towards the ridge
R_{d}
. With the help of these two facts, we start the proofs of (a-d).

(a) We first show that the following claim: for all
t\geq 0
and the initial point
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
,

p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\leq\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)-\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\epsilon_{t},

(61)

where
\epsilon_{t}=\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}=o\left(\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)
. By the differentiability of
p
guaranteed by condition (A1) and Taylor’s theorem, we have that

\displaystyle p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})+\frac{1}{2}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})+\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\left(V_{\diamond}(\bm{x}^{(t)}),V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)\begin{pmatrix}\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\lambda_{D}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}V_{\diamond}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\\
V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\end{pmatrix}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})+\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})}{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})+\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left(\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})+\beta_{0}\right)}{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\leq}}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})-\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left(\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})+\beta_{0}\right)}{2}\cdot\beta_{2}^{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{4}+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(v)}}}{{\leq}}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})-\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3},

where we use the equality
\bm{I}_{D}=V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}+U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})
in (i) and (iii), leverage conditions (A2) and (A4) to obtain that
\lambda_{D}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\leq\cdots\leq\lambda_{d+1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\leq-\beta_{0}
and
\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}
in (ii), apply the quadratic bound on
\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}
from condition (A4) to obtain (iv), and use the fact that
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\rho
in (v). We also use the fact that
\max\left\{\beta_{0},|\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}^{(t)})|\right\}\leq D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
and
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\rho
in (v). Claim (
61
) thus follows.

Given
Fact 2
and any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
,

\displaystyle p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-p(\bm{x}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-p(\bm{x}^{*})+p(\bm{x}^{*})-p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\left[\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\frac{1}{2D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2},

where we apply (
57
) to obtain the inequality. It implies that

\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\leq 2D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right].

(62)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}+\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+2\eta\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right)+\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+2\eta\Bigg[p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-p(\bm{x}^{*})-\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}\Bigg]+\eta^{2}\cdot 2D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\left(1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-2\eta\left(1-\eta D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)\underbrace{\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right]}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

whenever
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
, where we apply (
61
) and (
62
) in (i) and use the choice of
r_{2}>0
to argue that

\displaystyle\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\beta_{2}^{2}\rho+\frac{D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)r_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta_{0}}{8}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

in (ii). By telescoping, we conclude that when
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{2})
,

\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}\right)^{\frac{t}{2}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}.

The result follows.

(b) The result follows easily from (a) and the inequality
d_{E}(\bm{x}^{(t)},R_{d})\leq\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}
for all
t\geq 0
.

(c) The proof here is partially inspired by the proof of Theorem 2 in
Balakrishnan et al. 2017
. We write the spectral decompositions of
\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})
and
\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{x})
as

\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})=V(\bm{x})\Lambda(\bm{x})V(\bm{x})^{T}\quad\text{ and }\quad\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{x})=\widehat{V}(\bm{x})\widehat{\Lambda}(\bm{x})\widehat{V}(\bm{x})^{T},

where
\Lambda(\bm{x})=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\lambda_{1}(\bm{x}),...,\lambda_{D}(\bm{x})\right]
and
\widehat{V}(\bm{x})=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\widehat{\lambda}_{1}(\bm{x}),...,\widehat{\lambda}_{D}(\bm{x})\right]
.
By Weyl’s theorem (Theorem 4.3.1 in
Horn and Johnson 2012
) and uniform bounds (
16
), we know that for any
j=1,...,D
,

\displaystyle|\lambda_{j}(\bm{x})-\widehat{\lambda}_{j}(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j}\left|\lambda_{j}\left(\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})-\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{x})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x})-\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq D\left|\left|p-\widehat{p}_{n}\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}}\right).

Thus,
\widehat{p}_{n}
satisfies the first two inequalities in condition (A2) when
h
is sufficiently small and
\frac{nh^{D+4}}{|\log h|}
is sufficiently large. According to the Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
here) and uniform bounds (
16
),

\displaystyle\left|\left|G_{d}(\bm{y})-\widehat{G}_{d}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{y})V_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{y})-\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\left[V_{d}(\bm{y})V_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}-\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\right]\nabla p(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\left[\nabla p(\bm{y})-\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{y})\right]\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|\nabla\nabla p(\bm{y})-\nabla\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{\max}\cdot\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}+\left|\left|\nabla p(\bm{y})-\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|p-\widehat{p}_{n}\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\sqrt{D}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(1)}}{\beta_{0}}+\sqrt{D}\left|\left|p-\widehat{p}_{n}\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(1)}

\displaystyle\equiv\epsilon_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}}\right)

for any
\bm{y}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{2}
, where we use (
60
) and the fact that
\left|\left|\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}=1
to obtain (i). Hence, when
h\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{D+4}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
,

\left|\left|G_{d}(\bm{y})-\widehat{G}_{d}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\epsilon_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}}\right)\leq\frac{(1-\Upsilon)r_{2}}{\eta}

(63)

with probability tending to 1.

We now claim that
\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq r_{2}
and

\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\Upsilon\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}

(64)

for all
t\geq 0
. We will prove this claim by induction on the iteration number. Note that when
t=1
, we derive from triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}+\eta\widehat{V}_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})\widehat{V}_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{p}_{n}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}+\eta V_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})V_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})^{T}\nabla p(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\eta\left|\left|G_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})-\widehat{G}_{d}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)})\right|\right|

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h},

where we apply the result in (a) to obtain the last inequality. Moreover, by the choice of
\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}
and (
63
), we are guaranteed that
\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq r_{2}
. In the induction from
t\mapsto t+1
, we suppose that
\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq r_{2}
and the claim (
64
) holds at iteration
t
. The same argument then implies that the claim (
64
) holds for iteration
t+1
and that
\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq r_{2}
. The claim (
64
) is thus proved.

Now, given that
\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\beta_{0}\eta}{4}}<1
, we iterate the claim (
64
) to show that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t-1)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon\left[\Upsilon\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t-2)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}\right]+\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\left[\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Upsilon^{s}\right]\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+\frac{\eta\cdot\epsilon_{n,h}}{1-\Upsilon}

\displaystyle=\Upsilon^{t}\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(0)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}\right),

where the fourth inequality follows by summing the geometric series, and the last equality is due to our notation
\epsilon_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{D+4}}\right)
. It completes the proof.

(d) The result follows easily from (c) and the inequality
d_{E}(\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)},R_{d})\leq\left|\left|\widehat{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}
for all
t\geq 0
.
∎

E
Discussion on Condition (A4)

In this section, we explore several avenues to derive condition (A4) based on some potentially weaker assumptions. Recall from Section
3.3
that condition (A4) requires the following:

•

(A4)
(
Quadratic Behaviors of Residual Vectors
) We assume that the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
with step size
0<\eta\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\beta_{0}},\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\right\}
and
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
as its limiting point satisfies that

\displaystyle\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

for some constant
\beta_{2}>0
, where
\beta_{0}>0
is the constant defined in condition (A2).

E.1
Self-Contractedness Assumption

One important assumption that connects condition (A4) with the existing conditions (A1-3) in Section
3.1
is the so-called self-contracted property
(
Daniilidis
et al. 2010
;
Daniilidis
et al. 2015
;
Gupta et al. 2021
)
:

•

(A5)
(
Self-Contractedness
) We assume that the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
satisfies that

\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t_{3})}-\bm{x}^{(t_{2})}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t_{3})}-\bm{x}^{(t_{1})}\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ for all }\quad 0\leq t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{3}.

Condition (A5) requires the SCGA sequence to move towards the ridge
R_{d}
under a relatively straight and shrinking path. As we have proved in Proposition
3.3
that the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
converges to
R_{d}
when the sequence is initialized near
R_{d}
and its step size is small, condition (A5) is indeed a mild assumption as long as the sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
does not move erratically around
R_{d}
. More importantly, we demonstrate by Proposition
E.1
below that condition (A5) can be implied by a subspace constrained version of the concavity assumption on the objective (density) function
p
.

Proposition E.1
.

Assume condition (A1) and the following assumption on the objective function
p
:

•

(A6)
(
Subspace Constrained Concavity
) For any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{4}
with
r_{4}>0
being a constant radius, it holds that

p(\bm{y})-p(\bm{x})\leq\nabla p(\bm{x})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}(\bm{y}-\bm{x}).

Then, the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
defined in (
23
) with step size
0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
and initial point
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{4}
is self-contracted.

Notice that the density function (
24
) satisfies the “subspace constrained concavity” condition (A6) around a small neighborhood of its ridge
R_{1}
. Moreover, it is intuitive to verify that condition (A6) is a weaker assumption compared to our established “subspace constrained strong concavity” in Theorem
3.6
; see also Remark
3.3
.

The proof is inspired by Lemma 14 in
Gupta et al. 2021
. We show the self-contractedness for
s_{3}=t
as follows, where
t>0
is arbitrary. For all
s<t
and
0<\eta\leq\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
with
\bm{x}^{(0)}\in R_{d}\oplus r_{4}
, we calculate that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}+\eta\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+2\eta\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}(\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+2\eta\left[p(\bm{x}^{(s)})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right]+\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+2\eta\left[p(\bm{x}^{(s)})-p(\bm{x}^{(s+1)})\right]+\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-2\eta\cdot\eta\left(1-\frac{\eta D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\eta^{2}\cdot\left(\eta D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}-1\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2},

where we apply condition (A6) in inequality (i), use the ascending property of
p
from (a) of Proposition
3.3
to argue that
p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\geq p(\bm{x}^{(s+1)})
in inequality (ii), and leverage the inequality (
58
) guaranteed by condition (A1) to obtain (iii). The self-contractedness of the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
thus follows.
∎

Under the self-contractedness condition (A5), we argue by the following lemma that the existing conditions (A1-3) in the literature
(
Genovese et al. 2014
;
Chen et al. 2015
)
is nearly sufficient to imply the quadratic behavior of the residual vector
U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})
along the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. In other words, condition (A4) and the linear convergence of the SCGA algorithm hold without any extra assumption.

Lemma E.2
.

Assume condition (A5) throughout the lemma.

(a)

The total length of the SCGA trajectory is of the linear order,
i.e.
,

\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq 2^{10D^{2}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ for any }t\geq 0.

(b)

We further assume conditions (A1-2). Then,

\displaystyle\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\beta_{0}}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\beta_{0}}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

for any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{5})
with some radius
0<r_{5}<\rho
, where we recall that
\rho>0
is the effective radius in condition (A2) under which the underlying density
p
has an eigengap
\beta_{0}>0
between the
d
-th and
(d+1)
-th eigenvalues of its Hessian matrix
\nabla\nabla p
.

(a) This result follows directly from Theorem 15 of
Gupta et al. 2021
. Note that although their results are stated for the standard gradient descent path, the associated proof only utilizes the self-contractedness property of the iterative path. Thus, their proofs are applicable to our SCGA setting under condition (A5).

Fig 11:
Decomposition of the vector
\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}
into the summation
\sum\limits_{s=t}^{\infty}\left(\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right)
of subspace constrained gradient ascent iterative vectors.

(b) We first decompose the vector
\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}
into an infinite sum of SCGA iterations
\bm{x}^{(s+1)}=\bm{x}^{(s)}+\eta\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})
for
s\geq t
and obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\\
&=U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\cdot\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left(\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right)\\
&=\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\cdot\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\cdot\eta\left[V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}-V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\right]V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\end{split}

(65)

for any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in R_{d}\oplus\rho
, where we leverage the orthogonality between
U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})
and
V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}
and the idempotence of
V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}
for all
s\geq t
in (ii). See also Figure
11
for a graphical illustration of the decomposition.
By Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
and (
60
) here) and conditions (A1-2), we deduce that for all
s\geq t
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}-V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})-\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}D^{3}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}}{\beta_{0}}\cdot\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}},

where we use the Taylor’s theorem in (i) as well as apply the self-contractedness condition (A5) and possibly shrink the radius
r_{5}>0
so that
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{(2-\sqrt{2})\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\sqrt{2D}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}}
in (ii). Hence, by (
65
) and the fact that
\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq t}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}-V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}\cdot 2^{10D^{2}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}},

implying the second bound in condition (A4) with
\beta_{2}=\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{\beta_{0}}
. In addition,

\displaystyle\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}}.

The results follow.
∎

According to (b) of Lemma
E.2
, condition (A4) will hold with
\beta_{2}=\frac{2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{\beta_{0}}
whenever

2^{10D^{2}+1}D^{\frac{5}{2}}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\leq\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{4}.

(66)

The choice of
\beta_{2}>0
is a valid constant under the differentiability condition (A1). More importantly, (
66
) is essentially the same assumption as the first inequality of condition (A3). Compared to the corresponding condition in (A3), the upper bound in (
66
) for
\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}
around the ridge
R_{d}
is only shrunk by a dimension-dependent factor
\frac{1}{D\cdot 2^{10D^{2}+2}}
.
As condition (A3) and (
66
) are local, this adjustment does not induce too much extra strictness on the underlying density
p
.

E.2
Subspace Constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz Inequality Assumption

We have demonstrated in Appendix
E.1
that the crucial condition (A4) is valid under the self-contractedness assumption on the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. Consequently, the linear convergence of the SCGA algorithm can be established by slightly modifying the common assumptions (A1-3) in ridge estimation. Nevertheless, the self-contractedness property of the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
does not always hold in practice, and it may only be implied by the subspace constrained concavity condition (A6) as proved in Proposition
E.1
.

Given the fact that the underlying density function
p
or its estimator
\widehat{p}_{n}
may not satisfy the subspace constrained concavity assumption in many practical applications of SCGA and SCMS algorithms, we present another approach to deduce condition (A4) based on the well-known Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality
(
Polyak 1963
;
Lojasiewicz 1963
)
. Given any SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
with limiting point
\bm{x}^{*}\in R_{d}
and step size
0<\eta<\frac{2}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
, we consider the following condition:

•

(A7)
(
Subspace Constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz Inequality
) For all
t\geq 0
, there exists a constant
\beta_{3}>0
such that

\frac{1}{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\geq\beta_{3}\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right].

Similar to the standard Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality, there exist some objective functions that satisfy the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality but fail to be concave in the subspace constrained sense as in condition (A6); see
Charles and
Papailiopoulos 2018
;
Fazel et al. 2018
and Equation (36) in
Chen 2020
. From this aspect, condition (A7) incorporates some extra SCGA sequences satisfying condition (A4) and converging linearly to the ridge
R_{d}
. However, as the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality does not imply condition (A5) or (A6), it should not be regarded as a more general condition. Furthermore, unlike the standard gradient ascent/descent method (Theorem 2 in
Karimi et al. 2016
), the error bound condition (
i.e.
, Equation (
59
) here) does not imply the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality, indicating a challenge in validating condition (A7) in practice.

Despite these disadvantages, the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality condition does give rise to a concise proof for the linear convergence of the objective function value
\big\{p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
along the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
.

Proposition E.3
.

Assume conditions (A1) and (A7). Then, for any SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
with step size
0<\eta<\min\left\{\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}},\frac{1}{\beta_{3}}\right\}
, we have that

p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\leq\big[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(0)})\big]\cdot(1-\eta\beta_{3})^{t}.

The proof is inspired by Theorem 1 in
Karimi et al. 2016
. From (
58
) and condition (A7), we know that

\displaystyle p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\eta\left(1-\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\eta}{2}\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\geq\eta\beta_{3}\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right]

for all
t\geq 0
when
0<\eta<\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
. By some rearrangements, we conclude that

p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})\leq(1-\eta\beta_{3})\left[p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right].

The final display follows from telescoping.
∎

More importantly, the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality controls the total length of the SCGA path to be of the linear order and implicates the quadratic behaviors of residual vectors as required by condition (A4).

Lemma E.4
.

Assume conditions (A1) and (A7) throughout the lemma.

(a)

The total length of the SCGA trajectory is of the linear order,
i.e.
,

\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{4D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ for any }t\geq 0.

(b)

We further assume condition (A2). Then,

\displaystyle\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}}

for any
\bm{x}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{D}(\bm{x}^{*},r_{6})
with some radius
0<r_{6}<\rho
, where we recall that
\rho>0
is the effective radius in condition (A2) under which the underlying density
p
has an eigengap
\beta_{0}>0
between the
d
-th and
(d+1)
-th eigenvalues of its Hessian matrix
\nabla\nabla p
.

(a) This part of the proof is inspired by the arguments in Theorem 9 of
Gupta et al. 2021
. Based on the proof of (a) in Proposition
3.3
under condition (A1), we know from (
58
) that

\displaystyle p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\eta\left(1-\frac{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\eta}{2}\right)\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\eta}{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

when
0<\eta<\frac{1}{D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
. Using this inequality and condition (A7), we derive that

\displaystyle\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}+\eta\cdot V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\frac{\eta}{2}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})}-\frac{\eta\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{4\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})}-\frac{\eta\sqrt{2\beta_{3}}}{4}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2},

where we use the inequality
\sqrt{a-b}\leq\sqrt{a}-\frac{b}{2\sqrt{a}}
to obtain (i) and apply condition (A7) in inequality (ii). Since
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}=\eta\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}
, some rearrangement of the above inequality suggests that

\sqrt{\frac{\beta_{3}}{8}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t+1)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})}-\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t+1)})}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta_{3}}}\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left[\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(s)})}-\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(s+1)})}\right]

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta_{3}}}\cdot\sqrt{p(\bm{x}^{*})-p(\bm{x}^{(t)})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{\frac{8}{\beta_{3}}}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\beta_{3}}}\cdot\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ by condition (A4)}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})-\underbrace{V_{d}(\bm{x}^{*})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{*})}_{=0}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|\sup_{\epsilon\in[0,1]}M(\bm{x}^{*}+\epsilon(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}))^{T}\left(\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{2}{\beta_{3}}\left(D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}+\beta_{0}-\beta_{1}\right)\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2},

where we leverage condition (A7) again in (i). In addition, to obtain inequalities (ii) and (iii), we recall from the proof of (d) for Lemma
C.1
that
M(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[V_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x})\right]^{T}=V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})
, in which the singular values of
V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{0}(\bm{x})
is bounded by
D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
and the singular values of
\sum_{i=1}^{d}T_{i}(\bm{x})V_{d}(\bm{x})\Lambda_{i}(\bm{x})
is bounded by
\beta_{0}-\beta_{1}\leq D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
. The result thus follows.

(b) This part of the proof is analogous to our arguments in (b) of Lemma
E.2
, except that the SCGA sequence
\big\{\bm{x}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is no longer self-contracted. For the completeness, we still repeat some arguments and highlight the differences here. By Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
and (
60
) here) and conditions (A1-2), we have that for all
s\geq t
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}-V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})-\nabla\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}D\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}}+\frac{\sqrt{2}D^{3}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}}{\beta_{0}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{4\sqrt{2}D^{2}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}}+\frac{16\sqrt{2}D^{5}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\frac{8D^{\frac{7}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}},

where we possibly shrink the radius
r_{6}>0
so that
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{(\sqrt{2}-1)\beta_{3}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}}{4D^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}}
to obtain inequality (ii). Notice also that, since
\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}
may not hold without the self-contractedness property, we use a looser bound

\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{4D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

from (a) to derive inequality (i). Therefore, by (
65
) and the fact that

\left|\left|\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}=\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s+1)}-\bm{x}^{(s)}\right|\right|_{2},

we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{s\geq t}\left|\left|V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}-V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\eta V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})V_{d}(\bm{x}^{(s)})^{T}\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(s)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8D^{\frac{7}{2}}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(s)}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}}\cdot\frac{4D\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{\beta_{3}}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{(t)}-\bm{x}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}},

which implies the second bound in condition (A4) with
\beta_{2}=\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}}
. Finally,

\displaystyle\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})^{T}U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\left(\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\bm{x}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}}.

The results follow.
∎

The results in (b) of Lemma
E.4
also imply condition (A4) with
\beta_{2}=\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{\beta_{0}\beta_{3}^{2}}
whenever

\frac{32D^{\frac{9}{2}}\left(\left|\left|p\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|U_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{x}^{(t)})\nabla p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}p(\bm{x}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\beta_{3}^{2}}\leq\frac{\beta_{0}^{2}}{4}.

(67)

Once again, the choice of
\beta_{2}
is feasible under condition (A1) and the upper bound (
67
) can be viewed as a variant of the first inequality in condition (A3). From this perspective, the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality (A7) also leads to an alternative assumptions for condition (A4) and the linear convergence of the SCGA algorithm.

Remark E.1
.

Note that the results in Proposition
E.4
can be generalized to the directional or arbitrary manifold cases under conditions (
A1-3
). First, the subspace constrained Polyak-Łojasiewicz inequality for the SCGA sequence
\big\{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
on
\Omega_{q}
or an arbitrary manifold can be modified as:

\frac{1}{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\geq\underline{\beta}_{3}\left[f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]\quad\text{ for some }\underline{\beta}_{3}>0\text{ and any }t\geq 0,

where
f
is the objective (density) function. Based on the proof of (a) in Proposition
4.4
and our arguments in (a) of Lemma
E.4
, it follows that the total length of the SCGA trajectory on
\Omega_{q}
or an arbitrary manifold is of the linear order,
i.e.
,

\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}\right)\leq\frac{4q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{\underline{\beta}_{3}}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)\quad\text{ for any }t\geq 0.

Second, to establish the quadratic bounds for
\left\langle\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle
and
\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}
, one can follow the arguments in the proof of (b) in Lemma
E.4
and leverage the two facts:

1. The tangent vector
\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
can be decomposed into an infinite sum of SCGA updates (
44
) on
\Omega_{q}
or an arbitrary manifold as:

\displaystyle\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\Gamma_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s+1)})\right).

See Figure
12
for a graphical illustration. This equation is valid because parallel transports preserve inner products and are linear.

Fig 12:
Decomposition of the vector
\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
within the tangent space
T_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}
into the summation
\sum\limits_{s=t}^{\infty}\Gamma_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s+1)})\right)
of parallel transported SCGA iterative vectors. Here, the blue curves on
\Omega_{q}
are iterative paths of the SCGA algorithm, while the green vectors are tangent vectors
\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s+1)})\in T_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}
after being parallel transported to
T_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}
.

2. Under conditions (
A1-2
), we know that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\cdot\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\Gamma_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s+1)})\right)\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\|\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\bigg[\Gamma_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}}^{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\underline{\eta}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\bigg]\Bigg\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\widetilde{A}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}-\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{A}\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}-\bm{x}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\sum_{s=t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=O\left(\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)

for some constant
\underline{A}>0
, where we leverage the fact that the vector field
