X(\gamma(t))=\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(t))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(t))^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})

with
\gamma(0)=\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}
and
\gamma(1)=\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}
has its variation
\left|\left|X(\gamma(t_{1}))-X(\gamma(t_{2}))\right|\right|_{2}
bounded by

\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)})^{T}-\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}=O\left(\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(s)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\right)

according to the Davis-Kahan theorem for any
0\leq t_{1},t_{2}\leq 1
. However, we are not sure if the self-contractedness condition can also be adaptive to the directional or general manifold cases, given that the arguments in Theorem 15 of
Gupta et al. 2021
are based on the Euclidean geometry.

F
Other Technical Concepts of Differential Geometry on
\Omega_{q}

\bullet

Taylor’s Theorem on
\Omega_{q}
. Given a smooth function
f
on
\Omega_{q}
, its Taylor’s expansion is often written as
(
Pennec 2006
)
:

f(\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}(\bm{v}))=f(\bm{x})+\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\bm{v}\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\bm{v}^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})\bm{v}+o\left(\left|\left|\bm{v}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\right)

(68)

for any
\bm{v}\in T_{\bm{x}}
, where
\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}:T_{\bm{x}}\to\Omega_{q}
is the
exponential map
at
\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}
. One may replace the exponential map with a more general concept called the
retractions
on an arbitrary manifold; see Section 4.1 and Proposition 5.5.5 in
Absil et al. 2008
.

\bullet

Parallel Transport
. When comparing vectors in two different tangent spaces
T_{\bm{x}},T_{\bm{y}}
on
\Omega_{q}
, we leverage the notion of
parallel transport

\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}:T_{\bm{x}}\to T_{\bm{y}}
to transport vectors from one tangent space to another along a geodesic. In addition,
\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}(\bm{v})
is a tangent vector in
T_{\bm{y}}
after being parallel transported from
\bm{v}\in T_{\bm{x}}
along a geodesic (or great circle) on
\Omega_{q}
. The parallel transport mapping
\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}
is a linear isometry along any smooth curve on
\Omega_{q}
,
i.e.
,
\left\langle\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}(\bm{u}),\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}(\bm{v})\right\rangle=\langle\bm{u},\bm{v}\rangle
for any
\bm{u},\bm{v}\in T_{\bm{x}}
; see Proposition 5.5 in
Lee 2018
or Proposition 1 in Section 4-4 of
do Carmo 2016
.

\bullet

Sectional Curvature
.
Sectional curvature
is the Gaussian curvature of a two-dimensional submanifold formed as the image of a two-dimensional subspace of a tangent space after exponential mapping; see Section 3-2 in
do Carmo 2016
for detailed discussions about the Gaussian curvature. It is known that a two dimensional submanifold with positive, zero, or negative sectional curvature is locally isometric to a two dimensional sphere, a Euclidean plane, or a hyperbolic plane with the same Gaussian curvature
(
Zhang and Sra 2016
)
.

\bullet

Geodesically Strong Concavity
. A function
f:\Omega_{q}\to\mathbb{R}
is said to be
geodesically concave
if for any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\Omega_{q}
, it holds that

f(\varphi(t))\geq(1-t)f(\bm{x})+f(\bm{y})

for any
t\in[0,1]
, where
\varphi:[0,1]\to\Omega_{q}
is a geodesic with
\varphi(0)=\bm{x}
and
\varphi(1)=\bm{y}
. When
f
is differentiable, an equivalent statement of the geodesic concavity is that (Theorem 11.17 in
Boumal 2020
):

f(\bm{y})-f(\bm{x})\leq\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle.

A function
f:\Omega_{q}\to\mathbb{R}
is said to be
geodesically
\mu_{g}
-strongly concave
if for any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\Omega_{q}
, it holds that

f(\bm{y})\leq f(\bm{x})+\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle-\frac{\mu_{g}}{2}\cdot d_{g}(\bm{x},\bm{y})^{2}.

G
Normal Space of Directional Density Ridge

Recall that we extend the directional density
f
from its support
\Omega_{q}
to
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\setminus\left\{\bm{0}\right\}
by defining
f(\bm{x})\equiv f\left(\frac{\bm{x}}{\left|\left|\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}}\right)
for all
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\setminus\left\{\bm{0}\right\}
. As we will refer to conditions (
A1-3
) frequently in the next three sections, we restate them here:

•

(
A1
)
(
Differentiability
) Under the extension (
27
) of the directional density
f
, we assume that the total gradient
\nabla f(\bm{x})
, total Hessian matrix
\nabla\nabla f(\bm{x})
, and third-order derivative tensor
\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})
in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
exist, and are continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
and square integrable on
\Omega_{q}
. We also assume that
f
has bounded fourth order derivatives on
\Omega_{q}
.

•

(
A2
)
(
Eigengap
) We assume that there exist constants
\underline{\rho}>0
and
\underline{\beta}_{0}>0
such that
\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{y})\leq-\underline{\beta}_{0}
and
\underline{\lambda}_{d}(\bm{y})-\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{y})\geq\underline{\beta}_{0}
for any
\bm{y}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
.

•

(
A3
)
(
Path Smoothness
) Under the same
\underline{\rho},\underline{\beta}_{0}>0
in (
A2
), we assume that there exists another constant
\underline{\beta}_{1}\in\left(0,\underline{\beta}_{0}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\cdot q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\bm{y})\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle d\cdot q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{\max}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\beta}_{0}\left(\underline{\beta}_{0}-\underline{\beta}_{1}\right)

for all
\bm{y}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
and
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
.

Recall that an order-
d
density ridge of a directional density
f
on
\Omega_{q}=\left\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1}:\left|\left|\bm{x}\right|\right|_{2}=1\right\}
is the set of points defined as:

\underline{R}_{d}=\left\{\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}:\underline{G}_{d}(\bm{x})=\bm{0},\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x})<0\right\}=\left\{\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}:\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})=\bm{0},\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x})<0\right\}.

(69)

Lemma
G.1
below shows that under conditions (
A1-3
), the Jacobian matrices
\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{(q-d)\times(q+1)}
and
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q-d)}
(
i.e.
, projecting the columns of
\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
onto the tangent space
T_{\bm{x}}
) both have rank
q-d
at every point on
\underline{R}_{d}
, and
\underline{R}_{d}
will be a
d
-dimensional submanifold on
\Omega_{q}
by the implicit function theorem
(
Rudin 1976
;
Lee 2012
)
.
Analogous to the discussion about the normal space of a Euclidean density ridge in Appendix
C
, we define

\displaystyle\underline{M}(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}

\displaystyle=\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}

\displaystyle=\left(\underline{\bm{m}}_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\underline{\bm{m}}_{q}(\bm{x})\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q-d)}.

Different from the Euclidean density ridge case, it is the column space of

\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\left(\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]^{T},\bm{x}\right)=\left(\underline{\bm{m}}_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\underline{\bm{m}}_{q}(\bm{x}),\bm{x}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q+1-d)}

(70)

that spans the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
within the ambient space
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
. It can be seen from our Remark
4.1
that the rows of

\nabla\Psi(\bm{x})=\begin{pmatrix}\nabla\left[\underline{\bm{v}}_{d+1}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]\\
\vdots\\
\nabla\left[\underline{\bm{v}}_{q}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]\\
2\bm{x}\end{pmatrix}

spans the normal space of the solution manifold
\underline{R}_{d}
; see also Lemma 1 in
Chen 2020
.
Consequently, the column space of
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\bm{x})=\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
spans the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
within the tangent space
T_{\bm{x}}
at each
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}\subset\Omega_{q}
.
The technique in pages 60-63 of
Eberly 1996
is still valid to argue that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\underline{\bm{m}}_{k}(\bm{x})\\
&=\left[\underline{\lambda}_{k}(\bm{x})\bm{I}_{q+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}{\underline{\lambda}_{k}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})}\cdot\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})+\frac{\bm{x}^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}{\underline{\lambda}_{k}(\bm{x})}\cdot\bm{x}^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\right]\underline{\bm{v}}_{k}(\bm{x})\\
&=\left[\underline{\lambda}_{k}(\bm{x})\bm{I}_{q+1}+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}{\underline{\lambda}_{k}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})}\cdot\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\right]\underline{\bm{v}}_{k}(\bm{x})\end{split}

(71)

for
k=d+1,...,q
, where we use the fact that
\bm{x}^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})=\bm{x}^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})=0
on
\Omega_{q}
under the extension of
f
as in (
A1
). Let

\displaystyle\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\bm{x})\right],

\displaystyle\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\frac{1}{\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})},...,\frac{1}{\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})}\right],

\displaystyle\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\left[\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})

for
i=1,...,d
. Then,

\underline{M}(\bm{x})=\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x}).

(72)

As in the Euclidean data case, the columns of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\left[\underline{M}(\bm{x}),\bm{x}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q+1-d)}
are not orthonormal, and we again leverage the orthonormalization technique in
Chen et al. 2015
to construct
\underline{N}(\bm{x})
that shares the same column space with
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
but has orthonormal columns. That is, under the condition that
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
has full column rank
q-d
at every point
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
(see Lemma
G.1
),

\underline{N}(\bm{x})=\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})\left[\underline{J}(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}

(73)

with the Cholesky decomposition
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\underline{J}(\bm{x})\underline{J}(\bm{x})^{T}
, where
\underline{J}(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1-d)\times(q+1-d)}
is a lower triangular matrix whose diagonal elements are positive. Finally, the non-uniqueness of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
will not affect our subsequent discussions about the properties of directional density ridges.

Lemma G.1
.

Assume conditions (
A1-3
). Given that
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
,
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\left[\underline{M}(\bm{x}),\bm{x}\right]
, and
\underline{N}(\bm{x})
are defined in (
72
) and (
73
), we have the following properties:

(a)

\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
and
\underline{N}(\bm{x})
have the same column space. In addition,

\underline{N}(\bm{x})\underline{N}(\bm{x})^{T}=\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})\left[\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})^{T}.

That is,
\underline{N}(\bm{x})\underline{N}(\bm{x})^{T}
is the projection matrix onto the columns of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
.

(b)

The columns of
\underline{N}(\bm{x})
are orthonormal to each other.

(c)

For
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
, the column space of
\underline{N}(\bm{x})
is normal to the (tangent) direction of
\underline{R}_{d}
at
\bm{x}
.

(d)

For
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
, the smallest eigenvalue
\lambda_{\min}(\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x}))=\lambda_{\min}(\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T})\underline{M}(\bm{x}))\geq\underline{\beta}_{1}^{2}>0
, and

\mathtt{rank}(\underline{M}(\bm{x}))=\mathtt{rank}\left((\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T})\underline{M}(\bm{x})\right)=q-d.

Moreover, all the nonzero singular values of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
are greater than
\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}>0
, and

\mathtt{rank}(\underline{N}(\bm{x}))=\mathtt{rank}(\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x}))=q+1-d.

Therefore,
\underline{R}_{d}
is a
d
-dimensional submanifold that contains neither intersections and nor endpoints on
\Omega_{q}
. Namely,
\underline{R}_{d}
is a finite union of connected and compact submanifolds on
\Omega_{q}
.

(e)

For all
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
,

\left|\left|\left[\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\bm{x})\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{1}^{2}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\left|\left|\left[\underline{J}(\bm{x})^{T}\right]^{-1}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{1}},1\right\}.

(f)

When
\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}
is sufficiently small and
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
,

\left|\left|\underline{N}(\bm{x})\underline{N}(\bm{x})^{T}-\underline{N}(\bm{y})\underline{N}(\bm{y})^{T}\right|\right|_{\max}\leq\underline{A}_{0}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)^{2}\left|\left|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\right|\right|_{2}

for some constant
\underline{A}_{0}>0
.

(g)

Assume that another directional density function
g
also satisfies conditions (
A1-3
) after the extension
g(\bm{x})\equiv g\left(\frac{\bm{x}}{\left|\left|\bm{x}\right|\right|}\right)
in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
, and
\left|\left|f-g\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}
is sufficiently small. Then,

\left|\left|\underline{N}_{f}(\bm{x})\underline{N}_{f}(\bm{x})^{T}-\underline{N}_{g}(\bm{x})\underline{N}_{g}(\bm{x})^{T}\right|\right|_{\max}\leq\underline{A}_{1}\cdot\left|\left|f-g\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}

for some constant
\underline{A}_{1}>0
and any
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
, where
\underline{N}_{f}(\bm{x})
is the matrix defined in (
73
) with directional density
f
.

(h)

The reach of
\underline{R}_{d}
satisfies

\mathtt{reach}(\underline{R}_{d})\geq\min\left\{\underline{\rho}/2,\frac{\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}^{2}}{\underline{A}_{2}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)}\right\}

for some constant
\underline{A}_{2}>0
.

This lemma is a direct extension of Lemma
C.1
to the directional data scenario; thus, its proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
C.1
.

The proofs of properties (a), (b), and (c) can be inherited from the corresponding ones in Lemma
C.1
with mild modifications and we thus omit them.

(d) We will prove that the
(q-d)
nonzero singular values of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
and
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q-d)}
are bounded away from 0. Recall that

\underline{M}(\bm{x})=\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})

with

\displaystyle\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x}),...,\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\bm{x})\right]

\displaystyle\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\frac{1}{\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})},...,\frac{1}{\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\bm{x})-\underline{\lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})}\right]

\displaystyle\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle=\left[\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]\cdot\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})

for
i=1,...,d
. Under condition (
A2
),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\sum_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ since }\left|\left|\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right|\right|_{2}=1

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|\left|\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\quad\text{ by (\lx@text@underline{A2})}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left|\left|\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\underline{\bm{v}}_{i}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ since }\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}=1

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d\left|\left|\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{\max}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\beta}_{0}-\underline{\beta}_{1}.

It shows that all the singular values of
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})
or simply
\sum\limits_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})
are less than
\underline{\beta}_{0}-\underline{\beta}_{1}
. Moreover, under condition (
A2
) again, all the
(q-d)
singular values of

\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})=\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})

are greater than
\underline{\beta}_{1}
.

By Theorem 3.3.16 in
Horn and Johnson 1991
, we know that all the singular values of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
and
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\bm{x})
are greater than

\sigma_{i}\left(\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{0}(\bm{x})\right)-\sigma_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\underline{T}_{i}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}_{i}(\bm{x})\right)\geq\underline{\beta}_{0}-(\underline{\beta}_{0}-\underline{\beta}_{1})=\underline{\beta}_{1}>0,

where
\sigma_{i}(A),i=1,...,q-d
are singular values of a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q-d)}
in their descending order. Therefore, the minimum eigenvalue of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x})
satisfies

\lambda_{\min}\left(\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x})\right)=\lambda_{\min}\left(\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T})\underline{M}(\bm{x})\right)\geq\underline{\beta}_{1}^{2}>0.

(74)

Now, given
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\left[\underline{M}(\bm{x}),\bm{x}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q+1-d)}
and
\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}
, we know that

\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})=\begin{pmatrix}\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x})&\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\bm{x}\\
\bm{x}^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x})&1\end{pmatrix}.

If we denote the orthonormal eigenvectors of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x})
by
\bm{v}_{\underline{M},1}(\bm{x}),...,\bm{v}_{\underline{M},q-d}(\bm{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{q-d}
, then

\begin{pmatrix}\bm{v}_{\underline{M},1}(\bm{x})\\
0\end{pmatrix},...,\begin{pmatrix}\bm{v}_{\underline{M},q-d}(\bm{x})\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}\\
1\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{q+1-d}

are the orthonormal eigenvectors of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
, whose eigenvalues are thus lower bounded by
\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}
due to (
74
). Hence,
\mathtt{rank}(\underline{N}(\bm{x}))=\mathtt{rank}(\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x}))=q+1-d
.

By the implicit function theorem and the extra constraint
\underline{R}_{d}\subset\Omega_{q}
,
\underline{R}_{d}
is a
d
-dimensional submanifold on
\Omega_{q}
. It also implies that
\underline{R}_{d}
cannot have intersections, because otherwise the intersected points will violate the rank condition.

Finally, we argue by contradiction that
\underline{R}_{d}
has no endpoints. Assume, on the contrary, that
\underline{R}_{d}
has an end point
\bm{x}_{0}
. Our preceding argument has shown that
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
, the derivative of
\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})
, is bounded. In addition,
\bm{x}_{0}\in\underline{R}_{d}
. However, this contradicts to the implicit function theorem indicating that
\underline{R}_{d}
is a
d
-dimensional submanifold on
\Omega_{q}
, because at the end point
\bm{x}_{0}\in\underline{R}_{d}
, there exists no local coordinate chart for
\underline{R}_{d}
defined on an open set in
\underline{R}_{d}
. The results follow.

(e) By the proof of (d), we already know that all the
(q-d)
nonzero singular values of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
and
\left(\bm{I}_{D}-\bm{x}\bm{x}^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\bm{x})
are greater than
\underline{\beta}_{1}>0
. Also, all the
(q+1-d)
nonzero singular values of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
are greater than
\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}
. Thus, the results follow easily from the argument of (e) in Lemma
C.1
.

Finally, the proofs of properties (f), (g), and (h) are essentially the same as the corresponding claims in
Chen et al. 2015
. We thus omitted them. For (h), the reader should be aware that we have extended the directional density
f
from
\Omega_{q}
to
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}\setminus\{\bm{0}\}
. In addition, it is the columns of
\underline{M}_{E}(\bm{x})
that span the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
in the ambient space, whose nonzero singular values are lower bounded by
\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}
. The proof of (h) can also be found in Theorem 3 of
Chen 2020
.
∎

H
Stability of Directional Density Ridge

H.1
Subspace Constrained Gradient Flows

This subsection is modified from Section 4 in
Genovese et al. 2014
for directional densities and their ridges on
\Omega_{q}
. A map
\varpi:\mathbb{R}\to\Omega_{q}
is a subspace constrained gradient flow with the principal Riemannian gradient
\underline{G}_{d}
if
\varpi(0)=\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}
and

\varpi^{\prime}(t)=\underline{G}_{d}(\varpi(t))=\underline{U}_{d}(\varpi(t))\cdot\mathtt{grad}\,f(\varpi(t))=\underline{V}_{d}(\varpi(t))\underline{V}_{d}(\varpi(t))^{T}\nabla f(\varpi(t)),

(75)

where the last equality follows from (
30
). Given the definition of the directional density ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
in (
69
), it consists of the destinations of the subspace constrained gradient flow
\varpi
,
i.e.
,
\bm{y}\in\underline{R}_{d}
if
\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\varpi(t)=\bm{y}
for some
\varpi
satisfying (
75
). It will be convenient to parametrize the subspace constrained gradient ascent path with
\varpi
by arc length. Let
s\equiv s(t)
be the arc length from
\varpi(t)
to
\varpi(\infty)
:

s(t)=\int_{t}^{\infty}\left|\left|\varpi^{\prime}(u)\right|\right|_{2}du.

Denote the inverse of
s(t)
by
t\equiv t(s)
. Note that

t^{\prime}(s)=\frac{1}{s^{\prime}(t)}=-\frac{1}{\left|\left|\varpi^{\prime}(t(s))\right|\right|_{2}}=-\frac{1}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}\left(\varpi(t(s))\right)\right|\right|_{2}}.

With
\gamma(s)=\varpi(t(s))
, we have that

\gamma^{\prime}(s)=-\frac{\underline{G}_{d}\left(\gamma(s)\right)}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}\left(\gamma(s)\right)\right|\right|_{2}},

(76)

which is a reparametrization of (
75
) by arc length. Note that
\gamma
always lies on
\Omega_{q}
because its velocity is within the tangent space
T_{\gamma(s)}
for every
s\in[0,\infty)
. Lemma 2 in
Genovese et al. 2014
justifies the uniqueness of
\gamma
passing through any particular point
\bm{x}\in\left((\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho})\setminus\underline{R}_{d}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
under conditions (
A1-3
). The (reversed) subspace constrained gradient flow
\gamma
can be lifted onto the directional function
f
, as we may define

\xi(s)=f(\varpi(\infty))-f(\varpi(t(s)))=f(\gamma(0))-f(\gamma(s)).

(77)

Sometimes, we may add the subscript
\bm{x}
to the curves
\varpi_{\bm{x}},\gamma_{\bm{x}},\xi_{\bm{x}}
if we want to emphasize that
\varpi,\gamma,\xi
start from or pass through the specific point
\bm{x}
.

To analyze the behavior of the subspace constrained gradient flow
\xi
lifted on
f
, we need the derivative of the projection matrix
\underline{U}(\bm{x})\equiv\underline{U}_{d}(\bm{x})
along the path
\gamma
. Recall that
\underline{U}(\bm{x})\equiv\underline{U}_{d}(\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x}))=\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}
. The collection
\left\{\underline{U}(\bm{x}):\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}\right\}
defines a matrix field: there is a matrix
\underline{U}(\bm{x})
attached to each point
\bm{x}
. As mentioned earlier, there is a unique path
\gamma
and unique
s>0
such that
\bm{x}=\gamma(s)
for any
\bm{x}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\setminus\underline{R}_{d}
. Define

\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}\equiv\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\frac{\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s+\epsilon)))-\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s)))}{\epsilon}=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))+tE_{s})-\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s)))}{t},

(78)

where
E_{s}=\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))=\bar{\nabla}_{\gamma^{\prime}(s)}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))
with
\bar{\nabla}
being the Riemannian connection on
\Omega_{q}
. Under conditions (
A1-3
),
\xi
has a quadratic-like behavior near the directional ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
, analogous to Lemma 3 in
Genovese et al. 2014
.

Lemma H.1
.

Assume that conditions (
A1-3
) holds. For all
\bm{x}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
, we have the following properties:

(a)

\xi(0)=0
,
\xi^{\prime}(s)=\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}
, and
\xi^{\prime}(0)=0
. Thus,
\xi(s)
is non-decreasing in
s
.

(b)

The second derivative of
\xi
satisfies
\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)\geq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}
.

(c)

\xi(s)\geq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\gamma(0)-\gamma(s)\right|\right|_{2}^{2}
.

The proof is adopted from Lemma 3 in
Genovese et al. 2014
.

(a) The first property
\xi(0)=0
is obvious from the definition (
77
). Then,

\displaystyle\xi^{\prime}(s)=-\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))^{T}\gamma^{\prime}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}\quad\text{ by \eqref{arc_length_deri}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\nabla f(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2},

since
\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))=\bm{I}_{q-d}
for all
\gamma(s)\in\Omega_{q}
. By the definition of
\underline{R}_{d}
in (
69
),
\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))=0
when
\gamma(s)\in\underline{R}_{d}
. Thus,
\xi^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\xi(s)
is non-decreasing in
s
.

(b) Note that

\displaystyle\left(\xi^{\prime}(s)\right)^{2}=\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\nabla f(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle=\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right]^{T}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right].

Differentiating both sides of the equation, we have that

\displaystyle 2\xi^{\prime}(s)\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{\prime}(s)^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right]^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right].

Since
\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{U}(\gamma(s))=\underline{U}(\gamma(s))
(idempotent), we have that
\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))=\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))+\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))
, and hence the second term on the right-hand side of the above equation becomes

\displaystyle\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right]^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}(\gamma(s))\left[\mathtt{grad}\,f(\gamma(s))\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}(\gamma(s))\nabla f(\gamma(s))+\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\nabla f(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle=2\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))

Thus,

2\xi^{\prime}(s)\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)=2\gamma^{\prime}(s)^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))+2\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s)).

By (a) and (
76
), we conclude that

\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)=-\frac{\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}+\frac{\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}.

(79)

Now, we will bound the two terms in (
79
), respectively. As for the first term
-\frac{\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}
, we notice that
\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))
is in the column space of
\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))
. Hence,

\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))=\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))\Lambda_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\gamma(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\right]\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s)),

where
\Lambda_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\gamma(s))=\mathtt{Diag}\left[\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\gamma(s)),...,\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\gamma(s))\right]
. Therefore, from condition (
A2
),

\displaystyle\frac{\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))\Lambda_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\gamma(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\right]\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))\Lambda_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\gamma(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\right]\leq-\underline{\beta}_{0}

and consequently,

-\frac{\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))^{T}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}\geq\underline{\beta}_{0}.

As for the second term
\frac{\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}
, we notice that
\underline{U}(\gamma(s))+\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))=\bm{I}_{q+1}
, where
\underline{U}^{\perp}\equiv\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}
, and
\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))=\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))
. Then,

\displaystyle\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle=\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))+\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))G_{d}(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle=\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s)).

However,
\left|\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|=0
. To see this, note that
\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{U}(\gamma(s))=\underline{U}(\gamma(s))
and it implies that

\displaystyle\quad\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))+\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))=\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))

\displaystyle\implies\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))+\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))=\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s)),

showing that
\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}_{\gamma(s)}\underline{U}(\gamma(s))=\bm{0}
. To bound
\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U^{\perp}}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))
, we proceed as follows. As before, we let
E_{s}=\frac{d}{ds}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))=\bar{\nabla}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\cdot\gamma^{\prime}(s)
. Then, by the Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
here),

\displaystyle\left|\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\Big|\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\left[\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))+tE_{s})-\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s)))\Big]\underline{U}(\gamma(s))\cdot\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\nabla f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\lim_{t\to 0}\frac{\left|\left|\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))+tE_{s})-\underline{U}(\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s)))\right|\right|_{2}}{t}\cdot\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\left|\left|\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\nabla f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|E_{s}\right|\right|_{F}\cdot\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}.

Note that
\left|\left|E_{s}\right|\right|_{F}\leq\left|\left|\bar{\nabla}\mathcal{H}f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{\max}\cdot\left|\left|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right|\right|_{2}\leq q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{\max}
, because
\left|\left|\gamma^{\prime}(s)\right|\right|_{2}=1
. Thus, from condition (
A3
),

\frac{\left|\nabla f(\gamma(s))^{T}\underline{\dot{U}}(\gamma(s))\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|}{\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|\nabla^{3}f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{\max}\left|\left|\underline{U}^{\perp}(\gamma(s))\nabla f(\gamma(s))\right|\right|_{2}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\leq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}.

Therefore,
\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)\geq\underline{\beta}_{0}-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}=\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}
.

(c) For some
0\leq\widetilde{s}\leq s
,

\xi(s)=\xi(0)+s\xi^{\prime}(0)+\frac{s^{2}}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{s})=\frac{s^{2}}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(\widetilde{s})\geq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}s^{2}}{4}

by (a) and (b). As
\gamma
is parametrized by arc length, we conclude that

\xi(s)-\xi(0)\geq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}s^{2}\geq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\left|\left|\gamma(0)-\gamma(s)\right|\right|_{2}^{2}.

The result follows.
∎

The statement (c) in Lemma
H.1
is known as the quadratic growth condition in the optimization literature
(
Anitescu 2000
;
Drusvyatskiy and
Lewis 2018
)
. Under conditions (
A1-3
), such a quadratic growth of the subspace constrained gradient flow
\xi
lifted onto the directional density
f
enables us to quantify the stability of directional ridges under small perturbations on the directional density and develop the linear convergence of the (directional) SCGA algorithms on
\Omega_{q}
.

H.2
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We now show that if two directional densities
f
and
\widetilde{f}
are close, their corresponding ridges
\underline{R}_{d}
and
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
are also close. We will use, for instance,
\underline{\widetilde{G}}_{d}
and
\underline{\widetilde{U}}_{d}
, to refer to the principal (Riemannian) gradient and projection matrix with its columns as the eigenvectors corresponding to the smallest
q-d
eigenvalues of the (Riemannian) Hessian
\mathcal{H}\widetilde{f}
with the tangent space of
\Omega_{q}
defined by
\widetilde{f}
.

Theorem 4.1
.

Suppose that conditions (
A1-3
) hold for the directional density
f
and that condition (
A1
) holds for
\widetilde{f}
. When
\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}
is sufficiently small,

(a)

conditions (
A2-3
) holds for
\widetilde{f}
.

(b)

\mathtt{Haus}(\underline{R}_{d},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right)
.

(c)

\mathtt{reach}(\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})\geq\min\left\{\underline{\rho}/2,\frac{\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}^{2}}{\underline{A}_{2}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)}\right\}+O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}\right)
for a constant
\underline{A}_{2}>0
.

Our arguments are modified from the proof of Theorem 4 in
Genovese et al. 2014
as well as Proposition 4 and Theorem 5 in
Chen 2020
.

(a) We write the spectral decompositions of
\mathcal{H}f
and
\mathcal{H}\widetilde{f}
as

\mathcal{H}f=V\Lambda V^{T}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{H}\widetilde{f}=\widetilde{V}\widetilde{\Lambda}\widetilde{V}.

By Weyl’s Theorem (Theorem 4.3.1 in
Horn and Johnson 2012
), we know that

|\lambda_{j}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}|\leq\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f-\mathcal{H}\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{2}\leq q\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*},

where we recall that there are at most
q
nonzero eigenvalues of the Riemannian Hessian
\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})
on
\Omega_{q}
.
Thus,
\widetilde{f}
satisfies condition (
A2
). Moreover, since condition (
A3
) depends only on the first and third order derivative of
f
, they hold for
\widetilde{f}
when
\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}
is small enough.

(b) We present two methods based on two different flows to prove this statement and comment their pros and cons in Remark
H.1
.

Method A
: By the Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
and (
60
)),

\displaystyle\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}(\bm{x})-\underline{\widetilde{U}}_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}-\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})-\mathcal{H}\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{F}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}q\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}

for any
\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}
. Then, given that
\left|\left|\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}=1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\bm{x})-\underline{\widetilde{G}}_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})-\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}-\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\right]\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}+\left|\left|\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{\widetilde{V}}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\left[\nabla f(\bm{x})-\nabla\widetilde{f}(\bm{x})\right]\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}q\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\cdot\left|\left|\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}+\sqrt{q}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,1}^{*}.

Therefore, by the differentiability of
f
from (
A1
) and the compactness of
\Omega_{q}
, we obtain from the above calculations that

\sup_{\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}}\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\bm{x})-\underline{\widetilde{G}}_{d}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\leq C_{1}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}

for some constant
C_{1}>0
that only depends on the dimension
q
.

Now, let
\widetilde{\bm{x}}\in\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
. Then,
\left|\left|\underline{\widetilde{G}}_{d}(\widetilde{\bm{x}})\right|\right|_{2}=0
, and
\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\widetilde{\bm{x}})\right|\right|_{2}\leq C_{1}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}
. Let
\gamma
be the subspace constrained gradient ascent flow through
\widetilde{\bm{x}}
as defined in Section
H.1
so that
\gamma(s)=\widetilde{\bm{x}}
for some
s
. Note that
\gamma(0)\in\underline{R}_{d}
. From property (a) of Lemma
H.1
, we have that
\xi^{\prime}(s)=\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\widetilde{\bm{x}})\right|\right|_{2}
. Moreover, by Taylor’s theorem,

C_{1}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\geq\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\widetilde{\bm{x}})\right|\right|_{2}=\xi^{\prime}(s)=\xi^{\prime}(0)+s\xi^{\prime\prime}(u)

for some
u
between
0
and
s
. Since
\xi^{\prime}(0)=0
, from property (b) of Lemma
H.1
,

C_{1}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\geq s\xi^{\prime\prime}(u)\geq\frac{s\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2},

and consequently,
d_{g}(\gamma(0),\widetilde{\bm{x}})\leq s\leq\frac{2C_{1}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}
, where
d_{g}(\bm{x},\bm{y})
denotes the geodesic distance between
\bm{x}
and
\bm{y}
on
\Omega_{q}
. Therefore,

d_{E}(\widetilde{\bm{x}},\underline{R}_{d})\leq\left|\left|\gamma(0)-\widetilde{\bm{x}}\right|\right|_{2}\leq d_{g}(\gamma(0),\widetilde{\bm{x}})\leq\frac{2C_{1}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}.

Now let
\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}
. The same argument shows that
d_{E}(\bm{x},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})\leq\frac{2C_{1}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}
for some constant
C_{2}>0
because conditions (
A1-3
) hold for
\widetilde{f}
.

As a result,
\mathtt{Haus}(\underline{R}_{d},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})\leq\frac{2C_{1}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right)
.

Method B
: Since we are only required to bound the maximum Euclidean distance between
\underline{R}_{d}
and
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
,
i.e.
,
\mathtt{Haus}(\underline{R}_{d},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})
, we may view
\underline{R}_{d}
and
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
as solution manifolds in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
and tentatively ignore the manifold constraint
\underline{R}_{d},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}\subset\Omega_{q}
. Define
h(\bm{x})=\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}=\sqrt{\nabla f(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}
. Given that
\underline{M}(\bm{x})=\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}
, the gradient of
h(\bm{x})
,

\nabla h(\bm{x})=\frac{\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right]^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}{\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}}=\frac{\underline{M}(\bm{x})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})}{\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}},

(80)

is a vector in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
. Let
\bm{z}\in\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
. We define a flow
\phi_{\bm{z}}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
such that

\phi_{\bm{z}}(0)=\bm{z},\quad\phi_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(t)=-\nabla h(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t)).

It can be argued by Theorem 7 in
Chen 2020
that
\phi_{\bm{z}}(\infty)\in\underline{R}_{d}
when
\bm{z}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\delta_{0}
for some small
\delta_{0}>0
. In addition, we can always choose
\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}
to be small enough so that
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}\subset\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\delta_{0}
. By Theorem 3.39 in
Irwin 2001
,
\phi_{\bm{z}}(t)
is uniquely defined because the gradient
\nabla h(\bm{z})
is well-defined for all
\bm{z}\notin\underline{R}_{d}
. We can also reparametrize
\phi_{\bm{z}}(t)
by arc length as:

\gamma_{\bm{z}}(0)=\bm{z},\quad\gamma_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(s)=-\frac{\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))}{\left|\left|\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right|\right|_{2}}.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}=\inf\left\{s>0:\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s)\in\underline{R}_{d}\right\}
be the terminal time/arc-length point and
\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}})\in\underline{R}_{d}
be the destination of
\gamma_{\bm{z}}
on
\underline{R}_{d}
. The above argument also demonstrates that the flows
\phi_{\bm{z}}
or
\gamma_{\bm{z}}
converge to the manifold
\underline{R}_{d}
from the normal direction of
\underline{R}_{d}
, because we can write

\displaystyle\gamma_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}})=-\frac{\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))}{\left|\left|\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\right|\right|_{2}}=\sum\limits_{k=d+1}^{q}\bm{a}_{k}\cdot\underline{\bm{m}}_{k}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))

\displaystyle\quad\text{ with }\quad\bm{a}_{k}=-\frac{\bm{e}_{k-d}^{T}\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\nabla f(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))}{\left|\left|\underline{M}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\nabla f(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\right|\right|_{2}}

and the column space of
\underline{M}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))
spans the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
at
\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}})\in\underline{R}_{d}
.

The goal now is to bound
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}
because its length must be greater or equal to
\left|\left|\bm{z}-\pi_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\bm{z})\right|\right|_{2}
. We then define
\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(s)=h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))-h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))=h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))
. Differentiating
\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(s)
with respect to
s
leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\vartheta_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(s)=\frac{d}{ds}h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))&=\left[\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right]^{T}\gamma_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(s)\\
&=-\left|\left|\nabla h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right|\right|_{2}\\
&=-\frac{\left|\left|\underline{M}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))^{T}\nabla f(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right|\right|_{2}}{\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))^{T}\nabla f(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right|\right|_{2}}\\
&\leq-\lambda_{\min}\left(\underline{M}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))^{T}\underline{M}(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(s))\right)\leq-\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\end{split}

(81)

by (d) in Lemma
G.1
. (Note that
0<\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\leq\underline{\beta}_{1}
because
\lambda_{\min}(\underline{M}(\bm{x})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{x}))\geq\underline{\beta}_{1}
and by the continuity of
\lambda_{\min}(\underline{M}(\bm{y})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{y}))
, we can always choose
\delta_{0}>0
such that
\lambda_{\min}(\underline{M}(\bm{y})^{T}\underline{M}(\bm{y}))\geq\widetilde{\beta}_{1}
for all
\bm{y}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\delta_{0}
.)
As
\bm{z}\in\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
, by the proof of
Method A
, we know that

\displaystyle C_{1}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\geq\left|\left|\underline{G}_{d}(\bm{z})\right|\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{z})^{T}\nabla f(\bm{z})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(0))-h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))\quad\text{ since }h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}))=0

\displaystyle=\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(0)-\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}})\quad\text{ since }\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}})=0\text{ and }\vartheta_{\bm{z}}(0)=h(\gamma_{\bm{z}}(0))

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}\vartheta_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}^{*})\quad\text{ by the mean value theorem}

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\quad\text{ by \eqref{zeta_bound}},

where
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}^{*}
is some value between
0
and
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}
. Hence,
\mathcal{S}_{\bm{z}}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\widetilde{\beta}_{1}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right)
, which is independent of
\bm{z}\in\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
. This implies that

\sup_{\bm{z}\in\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}}d_{E}(\bm{z},\underline{R}_{d})\leq\frac{C_{1}}{\widetilde{\beta}_{1}}\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right).

We can exchange the role of
\underline{R}_{d}
and
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
and apply the same argument to show that

\sup_{\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}}d_{E}(\bm{x},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right).

In total, this leads to the conclusion that
\mathtt{Haus}(\underline{R}_{d},\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d})=O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,2}^{*}\right)
.

(c) By (h) in Lemma
G.1
, the reach of
\underline{R}_{d}
has a lower bound,
\min\left\{\underline{\rho}/2,\frac{\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}^{2}}{\underline{A}_{2}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)}\right\}
. Note that
\underline{\rho}
and
\underline{\beta}_{1}
depend on the first three order derivatives of
f
. Thus, the lower bound for the reach of
\underline{\widetilde{R}}_{d}
will be identical to the one for
\underline{R}_{d}
with an error rate
O\left(\left|\left|f-\widetilde{f}\right|\right|_{\infty,3}^{*}\right)
.
∎

Note that for the stability of directional ridges, one can relax the condition (
A1
) by requiring
f
to be
\beta
-Hölder with
\beta\geq 3
.

Remark H.1
.

We apply two different methods to establish the stability theorem of directional density ridges.
Method A
utilizes the subspace constrained gradient flow constructed in Section
H.1
and its quadratic behavior (Lemma
H.1
), while
Method B
defines a normal flow to the ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
induced by the column space of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
. Each of these two flows has its pros and cons. The subspace constrained gradient flow aligns more coherently with our directional SCMS algorithm (Algorithm
2
) to identify the (estimated) directional ridge from data, because it relies only on the first and second order derivatives of the (estimated) density
f
. Nevertheless, the subspace constrained gradient flow does not necessarily converge to
\underline{R}_{d}
in the optimal direction, that is, the normal direction to
\underline{R}_{d}
. This can be seen from the explicit formula (
72
) of
\underline{M}(\bm{x})
, which spans the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
. The normal flow

\phi_{\bm{z}}(0)=\bm{z},\quad\phi_{\bm{z}}^{\prime}(t)=-\frac{\underline{M}(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t))\underline{V}_{d}(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t))^{T}\nabla f(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t))}{\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t))^{T}\nabla f(\phi_{\bm{z}}(t))\right|\right|_{2}}

defined in
Method B
, however, converges to
\underline{R}_{d}
in its normal direction by construction. In general, the normal flow tends to the ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
faster than the subspace constrained gradient flow, but it may be complicated to compute in any practical ridge-finding task due to its involvement with third order derivatives of the (estimated) density
f
. Recently,
Qiao and Polonik 2021
presented explicit formulae for finding density ridges via such a normal flow and its discrete gradient descent approximation. Additionally, they defined a smoothed version of the ridgeness function that also circumvents the computations of third order derivatives of
f
.

I
Proofs of Proposition
4.3
, Proposition
4.4
, and Theorem
4.6

Proposition 4.3
.

Assume that the directional kernel
L
is non-increasing, twice continuously differentiable, and convex with
L(0)<\infty
. Given the directional KDE
\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})=\frac{c_{L,q}(h)}{n}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}L\left(\frac{1-\bm{x}^{T}\bm{X}_{i}}{h^{2}}\right)
and the directional SCMS sequence
\big\{\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}\subset\Omega_{q}
defined by (
39
) or (
40
), the following properties hold:

(a)

The estimated density sequence
\left\{\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is non-decreasing and thus converges.

(b)

\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=0
.

(c)

If the kernel
L
is also strictly decreasing on
[0,\infty)
, then
\lim\limits_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}=0
.

(a) The sequence
\left\{\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is bounded if the kernel
L
is non-increasing with
L(0)<\infty
. Hence, it suffices to show that it is non-decreasing. The convexity and differentiability of kernel
L
imply that

L(x_{2})-L(x_{1})\geq L^{\prime}(x_{1})\cdot(x_{2}-x_{1})

(82)

for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in[0,\infty)
. Then, with
\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})=-\frac{c_{L,q}(h)}{nh^{2}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{n}\bm{X}_{i}L^{\prime}\left(\frac{1-\bm{x}^{T}\bm{X}_{i}}{h^{2}}\right)
and the iterative formula (
40
) in the main paper, we derive that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)})-\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{L,q}(h)}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[L\left(\frac{1-\bm{X}_{i}^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}}{h^{2}}\right)-L\left(\frac{1-\bm{X}_{i}^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}}{h^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{L,q}(h)}{nh^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}L^{\prime}\left(\frac{1-\bm{X}_{i}^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}}{h^{2}}\right)\bm{X}_{i}^{T}\left(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\left(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle=\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\left[\frac{\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left[\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}-\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}\right]\cdot\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}}{\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}\frac{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{\left[\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}+\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}-\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right]}{\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}+\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}+\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\left(1+\sqrt{2}\right)\cdot\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

where we use the orthogonality between
\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}
and
\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}
in (i), multiply
\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}+\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}
to both the numerators and denominators of the two summands to obtain (ii), leverage the fact that
\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\geq\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}
in (iii), use the inequality
\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}
in (iv). It thus completes the proof of (a).

(b) Our derivation in (a) already shows that

\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\left(1+\sqrt{2}\right)\cdot\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left[\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)})-\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right]}.

Notice that, on the one hand, the differentiability of kernel
L
and the compactness of
\Omega_{q}
imply that
\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\leq B_{h,L}
for all
\bm{x}\in\Omega_{q}
, where
B_{h,L}>0
only depends on the bandwidth
h
and kernel
L
. On the other hand, our argument in (a) already proves the convergence of
\left\{\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
. Therefore,

\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\left(1+\sqrt{2}\right)B_{h,L}\cdot\left[\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)})-\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right]}\to 0

as
t\to\infty
. The result follows.

(c) Given the iterative formula (
40
) in the main paper, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\frac{\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\frac{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})+\left[\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}-\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}\right]\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}\frac{2\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{2\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-2\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}_{\leq 0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}},

where we leverage the orthogonality between
\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})
and
\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}
to obtain (i) and (ii). Under the assumption that the kernel
L
is strictly decreasing and (twice) continuously differentiable, we know that
\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}
is lower bounded away from 0 on
\Omega_{q}
. Therefore, with the result in (b), the above calculation indicates that

\left|\left|\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}}{\sqrt{\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}}}\to 0

as
t\to\infty
. The result follows.
∎

Remark I.1
.

The conditions imposed on kernel
L
in Proposition
4.3
is satisfied by some commonly used kernels, such as the von Mises kernel
L(r)=e^{-r}
. However, they can be further relaxed. On the one hand, it is sufficient to assume that the kernel
L
is twice continuously differentiable except for finitely many points on
[0,\infty)
. On the other hand, as long as the kernel
L
satisfies
C_{L,q}=-\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}L^{\prime}(r)r^{\frac{q}{2}-1}dr}{\int_{0}^{\infty}L(r)r^{\frac{q}{2}-1}dr}>0
and the true directional density
f
is positive almost everywhere on
\Omega_{q}
, Lemma
4.2
demonstrates that
\left|\left|\nabla\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\to\infty
with probability tending to 1 when
h\to 0
and
nh^{q}\to\infty
. Therefore, our upper bound on
\left|\left|\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)}-\underline{\widehat{\bm{x}}}^{(t)}\right|\right|_{2}
in our proof of (c) will be asymptotically valid for all
t\geq 0
, even without the strict decreasing property of kernel
L
. Under such relaxation, our conclusions in Proposition
4.3
are applicable to directional SCMS algorithms with other kernels that have bounded supports on
[0,\infty)
.

Proposition 4.4

(Convergence of the SCGA Algorithm on
\Omega_{q}
)
.

For any SCGA sequence
\big\{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}\subset\Omega_{q}
defined by (
44
) with
0<\underline{\eta}<\frac{2}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
, the following properties hold:

(a)

Under condition (
A1
), the objective function sequence
\big\{f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is non-decreasing and thus converges.

(b)

Under condition (
A1
),
\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=\lim_{t\to\infty}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right)=0
.

(c)

Under conditions (
A1-3
),
\lim_{t\to\infty}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d}\right)=0
whenever
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{1}
with the convergence radius
\underline{r}_{1}
satisfying

0<\underline{r}_{1}<\min\left\{\underline{\rho}/2,\frac{\min\left\{\underline{\beta}_{1},1\right\}^{2}}{\underline{A}_{2}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)},2\sin\left(\frac{\underline{\beta}_{1}}{2\underline{A}_{4}(f)}\right)\right\},

where
\underline{A}_{2}
is a constant defined in (h) of Lemma
G.1
while
\underline{A}_{4}(f)>0
is a quantity depending on both the dimension
q
and the functional norm
\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
up to the fourth-order (partial) derivatives of
f
.

The proof is similar to our arguments in Proposition
3.3
. For the completeness, we still delineate the detailed steps because the proof requires some nontrivial techniques, such as parallel transports and line integrals, on general Riemannian manifolds.

(a) We first derive the following property of the objective function
f
supported on
\Omega_{q}
, which is a counterpart of
Fact 1
in the proof of Proposition
3.3
.

\bullet

Property 1
. Given (
A1
), the function
f
is
q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-smooth on
\Omega_{q}
, that is,
\mathtt{grad}\,f
is
q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-Lipschitz.

This property follows easily from the differentiability of
f
guaranteed by condition (
A1
) and Theorem 4.34 in
Lee 2018
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left|\left|\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})-\Gamma_{\bm{x}}^{\bm{y}}\left(\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right)\right|\right|_{2}&\leq\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f(\widetilde{\bm{y}})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}^{-1}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\\
&\leq q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}^{-1}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\end{split}

(83)

for any
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\Omega_{q}
, where
\widetilde{\bm{y}}
lies on the geodesic curve
\varphi:[0,1]\to\Omega_{q}
with
\varphi(0)=\bm{x},\varphi(1)=\bm{y}
, and
\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})
. Then,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|f(\bm{y})-f(\bm{x})-\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\rangle\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\left|\int_{0}^{1}\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\varphi(t)),\varphi^{\prime}(t)\rangle dt-\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\rangle\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{=}}\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\Gamma_{\varphi(t)}^{\bm{x}}\left(\mathtt{grad}\,f(\varphi(t))\right),\Gamma_{\varphi(t)}^{\bm{x}}\left(\varphi^{\prime}(t)\right)\right\rangle dt-\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\rangle\right|\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{=}}\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\Gamma_{\varphi(t)}^{\bm{x}}\left(\mathtt{grad}\,f(\varphi(t))\right)-\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle\right|\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left|\left|\Gamma_{\varphi(t)}^{\bm{x}}\left(\mathtt{grad}\,f(\varphi(t))\right)-\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}dt\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\leq}}q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\int_{0}^{1}d_{g}(\varphi(t),\bm{x})\,dt\\
&\leq q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\left|\left|t\cdot\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\,dt\\
&=\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}^{2},\end{split}

(84)

where the equality (i) follows from the fundamental theorem for line integrals (Theorem 11.39 in
Lee 2012
), equality (ii) utilizes the isometric property of parallel transports, and inequality (iv) follows from (
83
). Moreover, since the velocity of the geodesic
\varphi
is always constant, we deduce that
\Gamma_{\varphi(t)}^{\bm{x}}\left(\varphi^{\prime}(t)\right)=\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})
and the equality (iii) follows.
We will make use of the following direction of the inequality (
84
):

f(\bm{y})-f(\bm{x})-\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle\geq-\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}.

(85)

Moreover, when
0<\underline{\eta}<\frac{2}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
,

\displaystyle f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=f\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\right)-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\,\eta\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\cdot\eta^{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\underline{\eta}\left(1-\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\eta}}{2}\right)\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\geq 0,

showing that the objective function
f
is non-decreasing along the SCGA path
\big\{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
on
\Omega_{q}
. Given the compactness of
\Omega_{q}
and the differentiability of
f
, we know that the sequence
\left\{f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is bounded. Thus, it converges.

(b) From (a), we know that when
0<\underline{\eta}<\frac{2}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
,

\displaystyle f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq\underline{\eta}\left(1-\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\eta}}{2}\right)\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{2-q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\eta}}{2\underline{\eta}}\right)d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right)^{2}.

Since the sequence
\left\{f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
converges, it follows that

\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=0\quad\text{ and }\quad\lim_{t\to\infty}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right)=0.

Recall from (
6
) that
\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\right|\right|_{2}=2\sin\left(\frac{d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\right)}{2}\right)
, so
\lim_{t\to\infty}\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\right|\right|_{2}=0
as well.

(c) Given condition (
A2
) and the fact that
\underline{r}_{1}<\underline{\rho}/2
, we know that

\bm{x}\in(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{1})\cap\Omega_{q}\text{ and }\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{x})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}=0\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\bm{x}\in\underline{R}_{d}.

Let
\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}=\pi_{\underline{R}_{d}}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})
be the projection of
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\Omega_{q}
in the SCGA sequence onto the directional ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
. Since
\underline{r}_{1}\leq\mathtt{reach}(\underline{R}_{d})
by (h) of Lemma
G.1
,
\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}
is well-defined when
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{1}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
. Recall from (
71
) that the column space of

\displaystyle\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\left(\bm{z}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\bm{z}^{(t)}\left(\bm{z}^{(t)}\right)\right)^{T}\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})\right]^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{(q+1)\times(q-d)}

coincides with the normal space of
\underline{R}_{d}
within the tangent space
T_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}
. We define a geodesic
\varphi:[0,1]\to\Omega_{q}
with
\varphi(0)=\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)},\varphi(1)=\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})
and calculate that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})-\underbrace{\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})}_{=0}\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\nabla\left[\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})\right]^{T},\varphi^{\prime}(\epsilon)\right\rangle d\epsilon\right|\right|_{2}\quad\text{ by Theorem 11.39 in \cite[cite]{\@@bibref{Authors Phrase1YearPhrase2}{Lee2012}{\@@citephrase{(}}{\@@citephrase{)}}}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\left|\left|\left\langle\underline{M}(\varphi(0)),\varphi^{\prime}(0)\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{1}\left[\left\langle\underline{M}(\varphi(\epsilon)),\varphi^{\prime}(\epsilon)\right\rangle-\left\langle\Gamma_{\varphi(0)}^{\varphi(\epsilon)}\left(\underline{M}(\varphi(0))\right),\Gamma_{\varphi(0)}^{\varphi(\epsilon)}\left(\varphi^{\prime}(0)\right)\right\rangle\right]d\epsilon\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\geq}}\left|\left|\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}-\left|\left|\int_{0}^{1}\left\langle\underline{M}(\varphi(\epsilon))-\Gamma_{\varphi(0)}^{\varphi(\epsilon)}\left(\underline{M}(\varphi(0))\right),\varphi^{\prime}(\epsilon)\right\rangle d\epsilon\right|\right|

\displaystyle\geq\left|\left|\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})^{T}\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\cdot\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{0}^{1}\left|\left|\underline{M}(\varphi(\epsilon))-\Gamma_{\varphi(0)}^{\varphi(\epsilon)}\left(\underline{M}(\varphi(0))\right)\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\varphi^{\prime}(\epsilon)\right|\right|_{2}d\epsilon

\displaystyle\geq\left|\left|\left[\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})\right]^{T}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\int_{0}^{1}\sup_{\epsilon\in[0,1]}\left|\left|\bar{\nabla}\underline{M}\left(\varphi(\epsilon)\right)\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\epsilon\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}d\epsilon

by Theorem 4.34 in
Lee 2018

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\geq}}\underline{\beta}_{1}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)-\frac{\underline{A}_{4}(f)}{2}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)\left(\underline{\beta}_{1}-\frac{\underline{A}_{4}(f)}{2}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)\right)
