\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{\underline{\beta}_{1}}{2}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)

where we utilize the isometric properties of parallel transports in (i), note that the velocity of geodesic is constant,
i.e.
,
\varphi^{\prime}(0)=\varphi^{\prime}(\epsilon)
for any
\epsilon\in[0,1]
to obtain (ii), leverage (d) of Lemma
G.1
to deduce (iii) and use the fact that
d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)\leq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{1}}{\underline{A}_{4}(f)}
when
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{1}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
in the inequality (iv). In particular for the inequality (iii),
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})
is a full column rank matrix and
\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})
lies within the column space of
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})
. Since the nonzero singular values of
\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})
are lower bounded by
\underline{\beta}_{1}>0
, it follows that

\left|\left|\left[\left(\bm{I}_{q+1}-\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\left(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)^{T}\right)\underline{M}(\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)})\right]^{T}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}\geq\underline{\beta}_{1}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right).

In addition, we also know that
\underline{A}_{4}(f)>0
comes from the supremum norm of
\bar{\nabla}\underline{M}(\bm{x})

over the geodesic connecting
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}
and
\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}
with
\bar{\nabla}
being the Riemannian connection, which in turn depends on the uniform functional norm
\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
of the partial derivatives of
f
up to the fourth order. By (b), we deduce that

\lim_{t\to\infty}d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{z}}^{(t)}\right)=d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d}\right)=0.

The results follow.
∎

The nonzero curvature structure of the unit (hyper-sphere)
\Omega_{q}
, on which the objective function (or density)
f
lies, induces an extra challenge in establishing the linear convergence of population and sample-based SCGA algorithms. Some useful techniques used in analyzing non-asymptotic convergence of first-order methods in
\mathbb{R}^{q+1}
, such as the law of cosines and linearizations of the objective function, would fail on
\Omega_{q}

(
Zhang and Sra 2016
)
. Therefore, we first introduce a practical trigonometric distance bound for the Alexandrov space
(
Burago et al. 1992
)
with its sectional curvature bounded from below.

Lemma I.1
(Lemma 5 in
Zhang and Sra 2016
; see also
Bonnabel 2013
).

If
a,b,c
are the sides (
i.e.
, side lengths) of a geodesic triangle in an Alexandrov space with sectional curvature lower bounded by
\kappa
, and
A
is the angle between sides
b
and
c
, then

a^{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|}c}{\tanh(\sqrt{|\kappa|}c)}b^{2}+c^{2}-2bc\cos(A).

(86)

The sketching proof of Lemma
I.1
can be founded in Lemma 5 of
Zhang and Sra 2016
. Note that the sectional curvature
\kappa=1
on
\Omega_{q}
. We inherit the notation in
Zhang and Sra 2016
and denote
\frac{\sqrt{|\kappa|}c}{\tanh(\sqrt{|\kappa|}c)}
by
\zeta(\kappa,c)
for the curvature dependent quantity in the inequality (
86
). One can show by differentiating
\zeta(\kappa,c)
with respect to
c
that
\zeta(\kappa,c)
is strictly increasing and greater than 1 for any
c>0
and fixed
\kappa\neq 0
. With Lemma
I.1
in hand, we are able to state a straightforward corollary indicating an important relation between two consecutive points in the SCGA sequence
\big\{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\big\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
on
\Omega_{q}
defined by (
44
):

\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}=\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right).

(87)

Corollary I.2
.

For any point
\bm{y},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}
in a geodesically convex set on
\Omega_{q}
, the update in (
87
) satisfies

\displaystyle 2\underline{\eta}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\nabla f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\bm{y}\right)^{2}-d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\bm{y}\right)^{2}+\zeta\left(1,d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\bm{y})\right)\cdot\underline{\eta}^{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2},

where
d_{g}(\bm{x},\bm{y})=\sqrt{\left\langle\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle}=\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{x}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}
is the geodesic distance between
\bm{x}
and
\bm{y}
on
\Omega_{q}
.

Recall that the (population) SCGA iterative formula on
\Omega_{q}
is given by
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}=\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)
. Note that for the geodesic triangle
\triangle\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\bm{y}
with
\bm{y}\in\Omega_{q}
, we have that

d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)})=\underline{\eta}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}=\underline{\eta}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}

and

\displaystyle d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)})\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\bm{y})\cdot\cos\left(\angle\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\bm{y}\right)

\displaystyle=\underline{\eta}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle.

By letting
a=\overline{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\bm{y}},b=\overline{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}},c=\overline{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\bm{y}}
, and
A=\angle\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)}\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\bm{y}
in Lemma
I.1
, we obtain that

\displaystyle d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\bm{y})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\zeta\left(1,d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\bm{y})\right)\cdot\underline{\eta}^{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\bm{y})^{2}-2\underline{\eta}\left\langle V_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})V_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\bm{y})\right\rangle.

Some rearrangements will yield the final display.
∎

Note that
\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
in our conditions (
A2-3
) is a geodesically convex set, where the minimal geodesic between two points in the set
\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
always lies within the set. Hence, Corollary
I.2
is applicable to our interested SCGA algorithm initialized within
\left(\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{\rho}\right)\cap\Omega_{q}
.

Theorem 4.6

(Linear Convergence of the SCGA Algorithm on
\Omega_{q}
)
.

Assume conditions (
A1-4
) throughout the theorem.

(a)

Q-Linear convergence of
d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
: Consider a convergence radius
\underline{r}_{2}>0
satisfying

\displaystyle 0<\underline{r}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\min\Bigg\{\underline{\rho}/2,\frac{\underline{\beta}_{1}^{2}}{\underline{A}_{2}\left(\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}+\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(4)}\right)},\frac{\underline{\beta}_{1}}{\underline{A}_{4}(f)},

\displaystyle 2\sin\Bigg[\frac{3\underline{\beta}_{0}}{8q\left(12\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\sqrt{q}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}\right)}\Bigg]\Bigg\},

where
\underline{A}_{2}>0
is the constant defined in (h) of Lemma
G.1
and
\underline{A}_{4}(f)>0
is a quantity defined in (c) of Proposition
4.4
that depends on both the dimension
q
and the functional norm
\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty,4}^{*}
up to the fourth-order (partial) derivatives of
f
. Whenever
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta(1,\underline{\rho})}\right\}
and the initial point
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
with
\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\in\underline{R}_{d}
, we have that

d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq\underline{\Upsilon}^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\quad\text{ with }\quad\underline{\Upsilon}=\sqrt{1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}}.

(b)

R-Linear convergence of
d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})
: Under the same radius
\underline{r}_{2}>0
in (a), we have that whenever
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta(1,\underline{\rho})}\right\}
and the initial point
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
with
\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\in\underline{R}_{d}
,

d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})\leq\underline{\Upsilon}^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\quad\text{ with }\quad\underline{\Upsilon}=\sqrt{1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}}.

We further assume (D1-2) in the rest of statements. Suppose that
h\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{q+4}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
.

(c)

Q-Linear convergence of
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
: Under the same radius
\underline{r}_{2}>0
and
\underline{\Upsilon}=\sqrt{1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}}
in (a), we have that

d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq\underline{\Upsilon}^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right)

with probability tending to 1 whenever
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta(1,\underline{\rho})}\right\}
and the initial point
\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
with
\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\in\underline{R}_{d}
.

(d)

R-Linear convergence of
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})
: Under the same radius
\underline{r}_{2}>0
and
\underline{\Upsilon}=\sqrt{1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}}
in (a), we have that

d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})\leq\underline{\Upsilon}^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right)

with probability tending to 1 whenever
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta(1,\underline{\rho})}\right\}
and the initial point
\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
with
\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\in\underline{R}_{d}
.

The proof is similar to our argument in Theorem
3.6
, except that the objective function
f
is supported on a nonlinear manifold
\Omega_{q}
here. The key arguments are credited to Corollary
I.2
. We first recall the following two properties.

\bullet

Property 1
. Given (
A1
), the function
f
is
q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-smooth on
\Omega_{q}
, that is,
\mathtt{grad}\,f
is
q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
-Lipschitz.

\bullet

Property 2
. Given conditions (
A1-3
), we know that
\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}>0\text{ for any }\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\big(\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}\big)\setminus\underline{R}_{d}
and

f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\right)\geq 0

for any
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
with
\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\in\underline{R}_{d}
.

Property 1
has been established in the proof of Proposition
4.4
, indicating that the objective function sequence
\left\{f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}_{t=0}^{\infty}
is non-decreasing when
0<\underline{\eta}<\frac{2}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
.
Property 2
is a natural corollary by Proposition
4.4
, because
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\text{Ball}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
and

f\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\right)

is the objective function value after one-step SCGA iteration on
\Omega_{q}
with step size
\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}
. The iteration will move
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}
closer to the directional ridge
\underline{R}_{d}
. With the help of these two properties, we start the proofs of (a-d).

(a) We first prove the following claim using Lemma
E.1
: for all
t\geq 0
and
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})&\leq\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}+\underline{\epsilon}_{t},\end{split}

(88)

where
\underline{\epsilon}_{t}=\left[2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right]=o\left(d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}\right)
.
By the differentiability of
f
ensured by condition (
A1
) and Taylor’s theorem on
\Omega_{q}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})^{T}\left[\mathcal{H}f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{=}}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle+\left\langle\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})^{T}\left(\underline{V}_{\diamond}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\begin{pmatrix}0&&&\\
&\underline{\lambda}_{1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})&&\\
&&\ddots&\\
&&&\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\underline{V}_{\diamond}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\\
\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\end{pmatrix}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle+\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\max\left\{0,\underline{\lambda}_{1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}}{2}\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle+\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left(\underline{\beta}_{0}+\max\left\{0,\underline{\lambda}_{1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}\right)}{2}\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{2}\left|\left|\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iv)}}}{{\leq}}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\left(\underline{\beta}_{0}+\max\left\{0,\underline{\lambda}_{1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}\right)}{2}\cdot\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)^{4}+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(v)}}}{{\leq}}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left[2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right]d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3},

where we leverage the equality
\bm{I}_{q+1}=\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}+\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})
in (i) and (iii), use conditions (
A2
) and (
A4
) that
\underline{\lambda}_{q}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\leq\cdots\leq\underline{\lambda}_{d+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})<-\underline{\beta}_{0}
and

\left\langle\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle\leq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}\left(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)^{2}

in (ii), apply the quadratic bound for
\left|\left|\underline{U}_{d}^{\perp}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right|\right|_{2}
in condition (
A4
) to obtain (iv), and leverage the facts that
\max\left\{\underline{\beta}_{0},0,\underline{\lambda}_{1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\}\leq q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}
and
d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\leq 2\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)
when
\left|\left|\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}-\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right|\right|_{2}\leq\underline{\rho}
in (v); recall (
6
). Our claim (
88
) is thus proved.

In addition, given
Property 2
and any
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{2}
, we derive that

\displaystyle f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\left[f\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}\left(\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right)\right)-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\Bigg[\left\langle\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\,\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}{2}\cdot\left|\left|\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2},

where we apply (
84
) to obtain the inequality. This indicates that

\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}\leq 2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left[f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]

(89)

for any
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{3}
. Therefore, by Corollary
I.2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-2\underline{\eta}\left\langle\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}),\mathtt{Exp}_{\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)}}^{-1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta\left(1,\underline{\rho}\right)\cdot\underline{\eta}^{2}\left|\left|\underline{V}_{d}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right|\right|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})^{2}+2\underline{\eta}\Bigg[f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{4}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt+\left(2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta\left(1,\underline{\rho}\right)\cdot\underline{\eta}^{2}\cdot 2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\left[f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(iii)}}}{{\leq}}\left(1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}\right)\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}-2\underline{\eta}\left[1-\underline{\eta}\cdot\zeta\left(1,\underline{\rho}\right)\cdot q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\right]\cdot\underbrace{\left[f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})-f(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\right]}_{\geq 0}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}\right)\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}

whenever
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta\left(1,\underline{\rho}\right)}\right\}
, where we utilize Corollary
I.2
and the monotonicity of
\zeta(1,c)
with respect to
c
in (i), apply (
88
) and (
89
) to obtain (ii), and use the choice of
\underline{r}_{2}
to argue that

\displaystyle\left(2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\underline{\beta}_{2}^{2}\arcsin\left(\underline{\rho}/2\right)+\frac{q^{\frac{3}{2}}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(3)}}{6}\right)d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}\cdot 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}}{8}\cdot d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})^{2}

in (iii). By telescoping, we conclude that when
0<\underline{\eta}\leq\min\left\{\frac{4}{\underline{\beta}_{0}},\frac{1}{q\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\cdot\zeta\left(1,\underline{\rho}\right)}\right\}
and
\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}\in\underline{R}_{d}\oplus\underline{r}_{2}
,

d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)})\leq\left(1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}\right)^{\frac{t}{2}}d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{\bm{x}}^{(0)}).

The result follows.

(b) The result follows obviously from (a) and the fact that
d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})\leq d_{g}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
for all
t\geq 0
.

(c) The proof is logically similar to the proof of (c) in Theorem
3.6
. We write the spectral decompositions of
\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})
and
\mathcal{H}\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})
as:

\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})=\underline{V}(\bm{x})\underline{\Lambda}(\bm{x})\underline{V}(\bm{x})^{T}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{H}\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})=\widehat{\underline{V}}(\bm{x})\widehat{\underline{\Lambda}}(\bm{x})\widehat{\underline{V}}(\bm{x})^{T}.

By Weyl’s theorem (Theorem 4.3.1 in
Horn and Johnson 2012
) and uniform bounds (
32
),

\displaystyle|\underline{\lambda}_{j}(\bm{x})-\widehat{\underline{\lambda}}_{j}(\bm{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f(\bm{x})-\mathcal{H}\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq q\left|\left|f(\bm{x})-\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{x})\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right).

Thus,
\widehat{f}_{h}
will satisfy conditions (
A2
) with high probability when
h
is sufficiently small and
\frac{nh^{q+4}}{|\log h|}
is sufficiently large. According to Davis-Kahan theorem (Lemma
D.1
here), uniform bounds (
32
), and the continuity of exponential maps, we have that

\displaystyle d_{g}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})\right),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\eta}C_{3}\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})-\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\eta}C_{3}\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\left[\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})-\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right]\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\underline{\eta}C_{3}\left|\left|\left[\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}-\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\right]\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}\underline{\eta}C_{3}\left|\left|\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})-\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}+\underline{\eta}C_{3}\cdot\frac{\left|\left|\mathcal{H}f(\bm{y})-\mathcal{H}\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}\cdot\left|\left|\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right|\right|_{2}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\underline{\eta}C_{3}\sqrt{q}\left|\left|\widehat{f}_{h}-f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(1)}+\underline{\eta}C_{3}\cdot\frac{q\left|\left|\widehat{f}_{h}-f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(2)}\sqrt{q+1}\left|\left|f\right|\right|_{\infty}^{(1)}}{\underline{\beta}_{0}}

\displaystyle\equiv\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right)

for any
\bm{y}\in\text{Ball}_{q+1}(\underline{\bm{x}}^{*},\underline{r}_{2})\cap\Omega_{q}
, where we utilize the Davis-Kahan theorem and
\left|\left|\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\right|\right|_{2}=1
in (i). Hence, when
h\to 0
and
\frac{nh^{q+4}}{|\log h|}\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&d_{g}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\bm{y})\right),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\bm{y}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})\underline{V}_{d}(\bm{y})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\bm{y})\right)\right)\\
&\leq\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right)\leq(1-\Upsilon)\cdot 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)\end{split}

(90)

with probability tending to 1.

We now claim that
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)
and

d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq\Upsilon\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}

(91)

for all
t\geq 0
. We again prove this claim by induction on the iteration number. Note that when
t=1
, we derive that

\displaystyle d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle=d_{g}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\right),\,\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(i)}}}{{\leq}}d_{g}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\underline{V}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\right),\,\underline{\bm{x}}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+d_{g}\left(\mathtt{Exp}_{\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\widehat{\underline{V}}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,\widehat{f}_{h}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\right),\,\mathtt{Exp}_{\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}}\left(\underline{\eta}\cdot\underline{V}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\underline{V}_{d}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})^{T}\mathtt{grad}\,f(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)})\right)\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(ii)}}}{{\leq}}\Upsilon\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h},

where we apply the triangle inequality in (i) and leverage the result in (a) and (
90
) to obtain (ii). The triangle inequality is valid in this context because the geodesic measures the minimal distance between two points on
\Omega_{q}
. Moreover, by the choice of
\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)}
and (
90
), we are ensured that
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)
. In the induction from
t\mapsto t+1
, we suppose that
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)
and the claim (
91
) holds at iteration
t
. The same argument then implies that the claim (
91
) holds for iteration
t+1
and that
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t+1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})\leq 2\arcsin\left(\underline{r}_{2}/2\right)
. The claim (
91
) is thus verified.

Now, given that
\Upsilon=\sqrt{1-\frac{\underline{\beta}_{0}\underline{\eta}}{4}}<1
, we iterate the claim (
91
) to show that

\displaystyle d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t-1)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon\left[\Upsilon\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t-2)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}\right]+\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\left[\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Upsilon^{s}\right]\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}

\displaystyle\leq\Upsilon^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+\frac{\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}}{1-\Upsilon}

\displaystyle=\Upsilon^{t}\cdot d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(0)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})+O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right),

where the fourth inequality follows by summing the geometric series, and the last equality is due to our notation
\underline{\epsilon}_{n,h}=O(h^{2})+O_{P}\left(\sqrt{\frac{|\log h|}{nh^{q+4}}}\right)
. It completes the proof.

(d) The result follows directly from (c) and the inequality
d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{R}_{d})\leq d_{g}(\widehat{\underline{\bm{x}}}^{(t)},\underline{\bm{x}}^{*})
for all
t\geq 0
.
∎
